The Parliament, in order to protect the inviolable and inalienable fundamental rights of human beings, as well as the country's independence, territorial integrity, economy, and national wealth, taking into account Hungary's international legal and European Union obligations, for the purpose of enforcing the exclusive punitive power of the state, enacts the following law:

## 1. §

(1) The perpetrator's criminal liability may only be established for an act which – with the exception of acts punishable under generally recognized rules of international law – was prescribed by law to be punished at the time of commission.

(2) No punishment may be imposed or measure applied for the commission of a criminal offense that was not provided for by law at the time of commission – or, in the case of application of Section 2 paragraph (2), at the time of adjudication.

## 2. §

(1) A criminal offense shall be adjudicated according to the criminal law in force at the time of its commission – with the exceptions set forth in paragraphs (2)–(3).

(2) If, according to the new criminal law in force at the time of adjudication of the act, the act is no longer a criminal offense, or is to be adjudicated more leniently, then the new criminal law shall be applied. *

(3) The new criminal law shall be applied retroactively in the adjudication of acts punishable under generally recognized rules of international law if such act was not punishable under Hungarian criminal law at the time of commission.

## 3. § (1) Hungarian criminal law shall be applied

a) to criminal offenses committed domestically,

b) to criminal offenses committed on Hungarian-flagged floating installations or Hungarian-flagged aircraft located outside the territory of Hungary,

c) to acts committed abroad by Hungarian citizens that constitute criminal offenses under Hungarian law.

(2) Hungarian criminal law shall be applied

a) also to acts committed abroad by non-Hungarian citizens if such act aa) is a criminal offense under Hungarian law and is also punishable under the law of the place of commission, ab) * is a criminal offense against the state, – except for espionage committed against allied armed forces and espionage against European Union institutions – regardless of whether it is punishable under the law of the place of commission, ac) is a criminal offense defined in Chapter XIII or XIV, or any other criminal offense the prosecution of which is prescribed by an international treaty promulgated by law,

b) also to acts committed abroad by non-Hungarian citizens to the detriment of Hungarian citizens, legal persons established under Hungarian law and other legal entities without legal personality, which are punishable under Hungarian law.

(3) In the cases specified in paragraph (2), the initiation of criminal proceedings is ordered by the Prosecutor General.

## 4. §

(1) A criminal offense is an act committed intentionally or – if this law prescribes punishment for negligent commission as well – through negligence, which is dangerous to society, and for which this law prescribes the imposition of punishment. *

(2) An act dangerous to society is an activity or omission that violates or endangers the person or rights of others, or the social, economic, and state order of Hungary according to the Fundamental Law.

## 5. §

A criminal offense is either a felony or a misdemeanor. A felony is an intentionally committed criminal offense for which this law prescribes the imposition of a punishment more severe than two years of imprisonment; all other criminal offenses are misdemeanors.

## 6. §

(1) Concurrence of criminal offenses exists when one or more acts of the perpetrator constitute multiple criminal offenses, and they are adjudicated in one proceeding.

(2) * It is not a concurrence of criminal offenses but a continuously committed criminal offense when the perpetrator commits the same criminal offense multiple times at short intervals, with a unified intent, to the detriment of the same victim.

(3) * If the perpetrator's multiple or continuous acts constitute one criminal offense, or multiple criminal offenses constitute one criminal offense pursuant to the provisions of this law, and an indictment has been filed against the perpetrator for this criminal offense, any further identical criminal offense committed by the perpetrator after the filing of the indictment shall be adjudicated as a separate criminal offense.

## 7. §

A person commits a criminal offense intentionally if he desires the consequences of his act, or acquiesces in such consequences.

## 8. §

A person commits a criminal offense through negligence if he foresees the possible consequences of his act but recklessly relies on their non-occurrence, or fails to foresee the possible consequences of his act because he fails to exercise the care or caution expected of him.

## 9. §

More severe legal consequences attached to the result as a qualifying circumstance of the criminal offense may be applied if the perpetrator is culpable of at least negligence with respect to the result.

## 10. §

(1) A person who begins the commission of an intentional criminal offense but does not complete it is punishable for attempt.

(2) The sentencing range for the completed criminal offense shall be applied to the attempt.

(3) The punishment may be mitigated without limitation or may also be waived if the attempt is committed on an unsuitable object, with an unsuitable means, or in an unsuitable manner.

(4) No punishment may be imposed for attempt,

a) if the completion of the criminal offense fails to occur due to his voluntary abandonment, or

b) if he voluntarily averts the occurrence of the result.

(5) If in the cases specified in paragraph (4) the attempt in itself already constitutes another criminal offense, the perpetrator may be punished for this criminal offense.

## 11. §

(1) If this law specifically so provides, a person who, for the purpose of committing a criminal offense, secures the conditions necessary for or facilitating it, invites to commit it, offers, undertakes, or agrees to commit it jointly, is punishable for preparation.

(2) No punishment may be imposed for preparation,

a) if the commencement of the commission of the criminal offense fails to occur due to his voluntary abandonment,

b) if he withdraws his earlier invitation, offer, or undertaking for the purpose of averting the commission, or endeavors to have the other participants abandon the commission, provided that the commencement of the commission of the criminal offense fails to occur for any reason, or

c) if he reports the preparation to the authorities before the commencement of the commission of the criminal offense.

(3) * If in the cases specified in paragraph (2) the preparation in itself already constitutes another criminal offense, the perpetrator may be punished for this criminal offense.

## 12. §

Perpetrators are the principal, the indirect principal, and the co-principals (hereinafter together: principals), as well as the instigator and the accomplice (hereinafter together: accessories).

## 13. § (1) A principal is a person who realizes the statutory elements of the criminal offense.

(2) An indirect principal is a person who realizes the statutory elements of the intentional criminal offense through the use of a person who is not punishable for this act due to childhood, morbid mental state, duress, or threat, or who is in error.

(3) Co-principals are persons who, with knowledge of each other's activities, jointly realize the statutory elements of the intentional criminal offense.

## 14. § (1) An instigator is a person who intentionally induces another to commit a criminal offense.

(2) An accomplice is a person who intentionally provides assistance to another in the commission of a criminal offense.

(3) The sentencing range established for principals shall also be applied to accessories.

## 15. § The punishability of the perpetrator or the punishability of the act is excluded or limited by:

a) childhood,

b) morbid mental state,

c) duress and threat,

d) error,

e) lawful defense,

f) necessity,

g) permission of law,

h) other reasons specified by law.

## 16. § * A person who has not reached the age of fourteen at the time of committing the punishable act cannot be punished, except

a) homicide [Section 160 subsections (1)–(2)],

b) homicide committed in a state of strong emotional disturbance (Section 161),

c) bodily injury [Section 164 subsection (8)],

d) violence against an official person [Section 310 subsections (1)–(3)],

e) violence against a person performing public duties [Section 311, if qualified pursuant to Section 310 subsections (1)–(3)],

f) violence against a supporter of an official person or a person performing public duties [Section 312, if qualified pursuant to Section 310 subsections (1)–(3)],

g) terrorist act [Section 314 subsections (1)–(2)],

h) robbery [Section 365 subsections (1)–(4)], and

i) plundering [Section 366 subsections (2)–(3)] the perpetrator thereof, if at the time of committing the criminal offense they had reached the age of twelve and at the time of commission possessed the necessary insight to recognize the consequences of the criminal offense.

## 17. §

(1) A person who commits the punishable act in such a pathological state of mental functioning that renders them incapable of recognizing the consequences of their act, or of acting in accordance with such recognition, cannot be punished.

(2) The punishment may be mitigated without limit if the pathological state of mental functioning limits the perpetrator in recognizing the consequences of the criminal offense, or in acting in accordance with such recognition.

## 18. §

Section 17 shall not apply to a person who commits the criminal offense in a state of intoxication or stupor resulting from their own fault.

## 19. §

(1) A person who commits the punishable act under the influence of coercion or threat due to which they are incapable of behavior in accordance with their will cannot be punished.

(2) The punishment may be mitigated without limit if the coercion or threat limits the perpetrator of the criminal offense in behavior in accordance with their will.

## 20. §

(1) The perpetrator cannot be punished for a fact of which they were not aware at the time of commission.

(2) A person who commits the punishable act in the erroneous belief that it is not dangerous to society, and has reasonable grounds for this belief, cannot be punished.

(3) Error does not exclude punishability if it is caused by negligence and this law provides for the punishment of negligent commission as well.

## 21. §

The act of a person who causes harm to the unlawful attacker with a defensive device installed for the purpose of preventing unlawful attack against their own or others' person or property, which is not capable of causing death, is not punishable, provided that the defender has done everything that can be expected in the given situation to ensure that the defensive device installed by them does not cause harm.

## 22. §

(1) An act that is necessary to avert an unlawful attack directed against, or directly threatening, one's own or another's or others' person, property or the public interest is not punishable.

(2) The unlawful attack shall be deemed as if it had also been aimed at extinguishing the life of the defender, if

a) it is committed against a person aa) at night, ab) armed, ac) having armed oneself, or ad) in a group,

b) it is an unlawful intrusion into a dwelling ba) at night, bb) armed, bc) having armed oneself, or bd) in a group, or

c) it is an unlawful intrusion armed into a fenced area belonging to a dwelling.

(3) A person who exceeds the necessary extent of the aversion out of fright or excusable emotional disturbance cannot be punished.

(4) The person attacked is not obliged to evade the unlawful attack.

## 23. §

(1) The act of a person who saves their own or another's person or property from an immediate and otherwise unavertable danger, or acts in this manner for the protection of the public interest, is not punishable, provided that the act does not cause greater harm than that which they sought to avert.

(2) A person who causes greater harm than that which they sought to avert because they do not recognize the magnitude of the harm out of fright or excusable emotional disturbance cannot be punished.

(3) Necessity cannot be established in favor of a person to whom the causing of the danger is attributable, or for whom the assumption of the danger is a duty by virtue of their occupation.

## 24. §

An act that is permitted by law or declared unpunishable is not punishable.

## 25. § Punishability is terminated by

a) the death of the perpetrator,

b) prescription,

c) pardon,

d) active repentance,

e) other grounds specified by law.

## 26. §

(1) Punishability – with the exception of those specified in subsections (2)–(3), and in the absence of a derogating provision of the law on the exclusion of prescription of certain criminal offenses – shall be prescribed after the passage of time corresponding to the upper limit of the sentence, but at least five years.

(2) * The punishability of the criminal offenses defined in Chapter XXVII shall be prescribed after twelve years.

(3) * The punishability shall not be prescribed of

a) the criminal offenses defined in Chapters XIII and XIV,

b) criminal offenses that are also punishable by life imprisonment, and

c) the criminal offenses defined in Chapter XIX that are punishable more severely than five years' imprisonment, if the victim of the criminal offense had not reached the age of eighteen at the time of committing the criminal offense.

## 27. § The starting date of prescription is

a) in the case of a completed criminal offense, the day when the statutory elements are fulfilled,

b) in the case of attempt and preparation, the day when the act fulfilling these ends,

c) in the case of a criminal offense that is realized exclusively by omission of the performance of a duty, the day when the perpetrator could still fulfill their duty without the consequence established in this law,

d) in the case of a criminal offense that consists in the maintenance of an unlawful state, the day when this state ceases.

## 28. §

(1) * The prescription is interrupted by a criminal procedural act taken against the perpetrator on account of the criminal offense by the court, the prosecution service, the investigating authority, or, in matters with an international aspect, by the minister responsible for justice or a foreign authority. On the date of interruption, the period of prescription begins again.

(1a) * If the victim of homicide committed in a state of strong emotional disturbance, intentional grievous bodily injury punishable more severely than three years' imprisonment, kidnapping, trafficking in human beings and forced labor, violation of personal liberty, or – with the exception set out in Section 26 subsection (3) point c) – a criminal offense against the freedom of sexual life and sexual morality had not reached the age of eighteen at the time of committing the criminal offense, the period until they reach or would have reached the age of twenty-one shall not be counted in the period of prescription.

(2) * If the criminal proceedings are suspended, the duration of the suspension shall not be counted in the period of prescription. This provision shall not apply if the criminal proceedings are suspended because the identity of the perpetrator could not be established in the investigation, they are at an unknown location, or they have become in a pathological mental state, furthermore if the accused at large is at a known location abroad and the criminal proceedings cannot be conducted in their absence.

(3) * The period during which, as a result of immunity based on the right of immunity, the criminal proceedings could not be initiated or continued because the right of immunity guaranteed by law was not suspended by the person entitled to decide, shall not be counted in the period of prescription. This provision shall not apply in the case of a criminal offense punishable upon private motion, for which the charge is represented by the private prosecutor.

(4) * In the case of probation, the duration of the probation period and the duration of reparation work shall not be counted in the period of prescription.

## 29. §

(1) A person shall not be punishable who, by the time of indictment, confessed to the commission of a misdemeanor or felony punishable by no more than three years of imprisonment against life, bodily integrity and health, against human freedom, against human dignity and certain fundamental rights, traffic-related, against property, or against intellectual property rights, and within the framework of a mediation procedure – or prior to it, but approved in an agreement concluded within the framework of the mediation procedure – made reparation for the harm caused by the criminal offense in a manner and to an extent accepted by the victim. This provision shall also apply if, among the criminal offenses in concurrence, the criminal offense against life, bodily integrity and health, against human freedom, against human dignity and certain fundamental rights, traffic-related, against property, or against intellectual property rights is the determining one.

(2) The punishment may be mitigated without limitation if, in the case of criminal offenses specified in subsection (1), the perpetrator confessed by the time of indictment to the commission of a felony punishable by no more than five years of imprisonment, and within the framework of a mediation procedure – or prior to it, but approved in an agreement concluded within the framework of the mediation procedure – made reparation for the harm caused by the criminal offense in a manner and to an extent accepted by the victim. This provision shall also apply if, among the criminal offenses in concurrence, the criminal offense against life, bodily integrity and health, against human freedom, against human dignity and certain fundamental rights, traffic-related, against property, or against intellectual property rights is the determining one.

(3) Subsections (1)–(2) shall not apply if the perpetrator

a) is a multiple or special recidivist,

b) committed the criminal offense in a criminal organization,

c) caused death by his or her criminal offense,

d) * committed the intentional criminal offense during the probation period of the suspension of imprisonment or after being sentenced to imprisonment to be executed for committing an intentional criminal offense, before the completion of the execution of imprisonment, or during the period of probation or conditional prosecutorial suspension, or

e) * previously participated in a mediation procedure for an intentional criminal offense, and as a result subsection (1) or (2) was applied against him or her, provided that two years have not yet elapsed from the date of the decision not subject to further appeal until the commission of the new intentional criminal offense.

## 30. § Criminal prosecution is precluded by

a) a private motion,

b) the absence of a complaint.

## 31. §

(1) In the cases specified in this Act, the perpetrator of a criminal offense shall be punishable only upon private motion.

(2) The victim shall be entitled to submit the private motion.

(3) * In the cases specified in Section 69 (5) of Act XC of 2017 on Criminal Procedure, the private motion may be submitted by the legal representative of the victim. The private motion may also be submitted independently by a victim who is a minor with limited capacity to act, and his or her legal representative shall also be entitled to do so. In these cases, the guardianship authority shall also be entitled to submit the private motion. In case of conflict of interest, the provisions of the Civil Code shall apply.

(4) If the victim entitled to submit the private motion dies, his or her relative shall be entitled to submit the private motion.

(5) A private motion submitted against any perpetrator shall be effective against all perpetrators.

(6) A private motion may not be withdrawn.

## 32. §

In the cases specified in this Act, the perpetrator of a criminal offense shall be punishable only upon a complaint made by the person entitled to do so.

## 33. § (1) Punishments

a) imprisonment,

b) detention,

c) community service work,

d) fine,

e) disqualification from occupation,

f) disqualification from driving,

g) expulsion,

h) disqualification from attending sports events,

i) expulsion from the country.

(2) Accessory penalty is disqualification from public affairs.

(3) Punishments – with the exceptions set out in subsections (5) and (6) – may also be imposed concurrently.

(4) * If the lower limit of the penalty range for the criminal offense does not reach one year of imprisonment, instead of imprisonment, detention, community service work, fine, disqualification from occupation, disqualification from driving, expulsion, disqualification from attending sports events or expulsion from the country, or more than one of these punishments, may be imposed.

(5) If this Act prescribes detention as punishment for a criminal offense, instead of or in addition to this punishment, community service work, fine, disqualification from occupation, disqualification from driving, expulsion, disqualification from attending sports events or expulsion from the country, or more than one of these punishments, may be imposed.

(6) The following may not be imposed:

a) detention or community service work in addition to imprisonment,

b) community service work or fine in addition to expulsion from the country,

c) * fine in addition to life imprisonment.

## 34. § Imprisonment is for a definite period or for life.

## 35. §

(1) If the court imposes imprisonment, it shall order its execution in a prison facility, correctional facility or penitentiary.

(2) Having regard to the circumstances relevant to the imposition of punishment, a degree of execution one level more lenient or one level stricter than that specified by law may be determined. This provision shall not apply to life imprisonment imposed pursuant to Section 44 (1)–(2) and Section 90 (2).

## 36. §

The shortest duration of definite imprisonment is three months, the longest duration is twenty years; in case of commission in a criminal organization, as a special or multiple recidivist, or in case of concurrence or aggregate punishment, twenty-five years.

## 37. §

(1) The degree of execution of the imprisonment penalty is prison facility if it was imposed for a misdemeanor, except when the convict is a recidivist.

(2) The degree of execution of the imprisonment penalty is correctional facility if it was imposed

a) for a felony, or

b) for a misdemeanor, and the convict is a recidivist.

(3) The degree of execution of the imprisonment penalty is penitentiary if

a) the imprisonment of three years or longer duration was imposed for aa) a criminal offense specified in Chapters XIII, XIV or XXIV, ab) a military criminal offense also punishable by life imprisonment, ac) * an act of terrorism [Section 314 (1)–(2), Sections 315–316/A], financing of terrorism [Sections 318 and 318/A], hijacking of a vehicle [Section 320 (1)–(3)], participation in a criminal organization [Section 321 (1)], misuse of explosives or explosive devices (Section 324), misuse of firearms or ammunition [Section 325 (1)–(3)], misuse of weapons prohibited by international treaty [Section 326 (1)–(6)], misuse of military technology products or services [Section 329 (1)–(4)], misuse of dual-use items [Section 330 (1)–(3)], ad) * aggravated cases of homicide, drug trafficking, possession of drugs, kidnapping, trafficking in human beings and forced labor, sexual violence, causing public danger, violation of international economic prohibition or robbery [Section 160 (2), Sections 176 (2)–(3), Section 177 (1)–(2), Section 178 (2), Section 179 (2), Section 190 (2)–(4), Section 192 (1)–(6), Section 197 (2)–(4), Section 322 (2)–(3), Section 327 (3), Section 365 (3)–(4)], or

b) * the imprisonment is of two years or longer duration, and ba) the convict is a multiple recidivist, or bb) the criminal offense was committed in a criminal organization.

## 38. §

(1) * In the case of imposing a fixed-term imprisonment, the court shall establish in the decision on the merits the earliest date of conditional release, or – in the cases defined in paragraph (4) – that the possibility of conditional release is excluded.

(2) If the possibility of conditional release is not excluded, its earliest date shall be

a) two-thirds of the sentence,

b) in the case of a recidivist, three-quarters thereof, but at least three months, the day following the completion thereof.

(3) * In the case of imposing imprisonment not exceeding five years – in cases deserving special consideration – the court may provide in the decision on the merits that the convicted person may be conditionally released after serving half of the sentence. This provision shall not apply if the convicted person is a multiple recidivist.

(4) The following persons shall not be conditionally released:

a) a multiple recidivist, if the imprisonment is to be executed at the level of fortress,

b) a violent multiple recidivist,

c) a person who committed the criminal offence in a criminal organisation,

d) a person who has been sentenced to imprisonment for an intentional criminal offence committed after their earlier sentencing to a fixed-term custodial imprisonment, before the completion of the execution or the termination of enforceability,

e) * a person who has been sentenced as a perpetrator to custodial imprisonment for the following criminal offences: ea) genocide, crime against humanity, apartheid, if committed by intentionally causing death [Section 142(1)(a), Section 143(1)(a), Section 144(1)(a)], eb) homicide [Section 160(1) and (2)], ec) kidnapping [Section 190(4)], ed) more severely qualified cases of prison riot, terrorist act, hijacking of a vehicle and mutiny, if committed by intentionally causing death [Section 284(4), Section 314(1), Section 320(2), Section 442(4)], or ee) violence against a superior or service officer [Section 445(5)(a)].

(5) * The following persons shall also not be conditionally released:

a) a person who has been sentenced to custodial imprisonment aa) for preparation in the case of committing a criminal offence defined in paragraph (4)(e), ab) as an accomplice, or ac) with the application of unlimited mitigation,

b) for a violent criminal offence against a person punishable by imprisonment of eight years or more severe, committed to the detriment of their relative, or

c) for a criminal offence against sexual freedom and sexual morality punishable by imprisonment of eight years or more severe, committed to the detriment of a person who has not attained the age of eighteen.

(6) * The court may provide in the decision on the merits on the conditional releasability of the perpetrator defined in paragraph (5), if in view of the circumstances of commission, the degree of danger to society inherent in the person of the perpetrator or other sentencing circumstances, the protection of society and the prevention of the perpetrator committing another criminal offence can be achieved by ordering probationary supervision and determining a separate rule of conduct.

(7) * For the purposes of paragraph (5)(b), relative shall also mean the parent of the perpetrator's child, their former spouse, their former cohabitant, their guardian, their ward, their legal guardian or their ward.

## 39. §

(1) * In the case of fixed-term imprisonment, the duration of conditional release shall be equal to the remaining part of the imprisonment, but at least one year. In the application of Section 38(3), the court may provide in the decision on the merits that the duration of conditional release shall be extended by at least one and at most three years.

(2) If the remaining part of the imprisonment is shorter than one year and its execution has not been ordered, the sentence shall be considered – after the expiry of conditional release – served on the last day of the remaining part.

## 40. § (1) * The court shall terminate conditional release if the convicted person

a) is sentenced to custodial imprisonment for a criminal offence committed during the period of conditional release after the decision on the merits has become final, or

b) is sentenced to custodial imprisonment for a criminal offence committed during the period of conditional release.

(2) The court may terminate conditional release if the convicted person is sentenced to other punishment in addition to those defined in paragraph (1).

(3) In the event of termination of conditional release, the time spent on conditional release shall not be counted into the imprisonment.

(4) * If during the period of conditional release an imprisonment imposed on the convicted person for a criminal offence committed before the earlier decision on the merits became final must be executed, the execution of imprisonment shall interrupt the conditional release, and the court shall postpone the earliest date of continuation of conditional release

a) until the date of conditional release granted from the subsequently imposed imprisonment, or

b) – if in the case of the subsequently imposed imprisonment the possibility of conditional release is excluded – for the duration of the execution of the imprisonment.

## 41. §

(1) * Life imprisonment may be imposed on a person who has attained the age of twenty at the time of committing the criminal offence. This provision shall also apply to life imprisonment that may be imposed pursuant to Section 90(2).

(2) The execution level of life imprisonment is fortress.

## 42. §

* In the case of imposing life imprisonment, the court shall determine in the decision on the merits the earliest date of conditional release, or shall exclude the possibility of conditional release.

## 43. §

(1) If in the case of imposing life imprisonment the court does not exclude the possibility of conditional release, it shall determine its earliest date at a minimum of twenty-five and a maximum of forty years. The earliest date of conditional release shall be determined in years.

(2) In the case of life imprisonment, the duration of conditional release shall be at least fifteen years.

## 44. § (1) In the case of imposing life imprisonment, the court may exclude the possibility of conditional release only for the following criminal offences:

a) genocide [Section 142(1)],

b) crime against humanity [Section 143(1)],

c) apartheid [Section 144(1) and (3)],

d) more severely qualified case of violence against a war envoy [Section 148(2)],

e) violence against protected persons [Section 149(1)–(2)],

f) use of weapons prohibited by international treaty [Section 155(1)],

g) other war crime (Section 158),

h) more severely qualified case of homicide [Section 160(2)],

i) more severely qualified case of kidnapping [Section 190(3)–(4)],

j) * more severely qualified case of human trafficking and forced labour [Section 192(6)],

k) violent alteration of the constitutional order [Section 254(1)],

l) more severely qualified case of destruction [Section 257(2)],

m) more severely qualified case of prison riot [Section 284(4)],

n) terrorist act [Section 314(1)],

o) more severely qualified case of hijacking of a vehicle [Section 320(2)],

p) more severely qualified case of causing public danger [Section 322(3)],

q) more severely qualified case of mutiny [Section 442(4)],

r) more severely qualified case of violence against a superior or service officer [Section 445(5)], if committed with violence against a person or violence against property.

(2) The possibility of conditional release shall be excluded if the perpetrator

a) is a violent multiple recidivist, or

b) committed the criminal offence defined in paragraph (1) in a criminal organisation.

## 45. §

(1) If the convicted person is sentenced to a definite term of imprisonment to be executed during the execution of the life imprisonment for an offense committed before the sentencing to life imprisonment, the court shall postpone the earliest date of conditional release until the duration of the definite term of imprisonment to be executed.

(2) If the convicted person is sentenced to a definite term of imprisonment to be executed during conditional release from life imprisonment for an offense committed before the sentencing to life imprisonment, the court shall revoke the conditional release and postpone the earliest date of conditional release until the duration of the definite term of imprisonment to be executed.

(3) If the convicted person is sentenced to a definite term of imprisonment during the execution of life imprisonment for an offense committed during the execution of life imprisonment, the court shall postpone the earliest date of conditional release until the duration of the definite term of imprisonment, but for a period of at least five and at most twenty years.

(4) If the convicted person is sentenced to a definite term of imprisonment during conditional release from life imprisonment for an offense committed during the execution of life imprisonment, the court shall revoke the conditional release and postpone the earliest date of conditional release until the duration of the definite term of imprisonment, but for a period of at least five and at most twenty years.

(5) If the convicted person is sentenced to a definite term of imprisonment for an offense committed during conditional release from life imprisonment, the court shall revoke the conditional release and postpone the earliest date of conditional release until the duration of the definite term of imprisonment, but for a period of at least five and at most twenty years.

(6) * If the postponement of the earliest date of conditional release from life imprisonment occurs due to the definite term of imprisonment under paragraphs (1), (2), (4) and (5), the earliest date of conditional release shall be determined taking into account the pre-trial detention and the period of criminal supervision credited to the definite term of imprisonment.

(7) * The convicted person may not be granted conditional release if they are sentenced to life imprisonment repeatedly. If the previous life imprisonment has not yet been executed, the repeatedly imposed life imprisonment may not be executed.

(8) * The court shall revoke the conditional release if the convicted person

a) during conditional release from life imprisonment, or

b) for an offense committed during conditional release from life imprisonment is sentenced repeatedly to life imprisonment.

## 46. §

(1) The duration of detention shall be determined in days, its shortest duration being five days, its longest duration being ninety days.

(2) Detention shall be executed in a penal institution.

## 47. §

(1) The duration of community service shall be determined in hours, its minimum measure being forty-eight hours, its maximum measure being three hundred and twelve hours.

(2) * The convicted person shall perform community service, unless otherwise provided by law, at least one day per week – on the weekly rest day or in their free time – without remuneration.

(4) A person sentenced to community service is obliged to perform the work specified for them. The offender may be required to perform work which – taking into account their health and qualifications – they can foreseeably perform.

## 48. §

(1) If the convicted person fails to perform the work specified for them through their own fault, the community service or the remaining part thereof shall be converted into imprisonment.

(2) The imprisonment replacing community service shall be executed in a penal institution, at the level of prison.

(3) The imprisonment replacing community service may be shorter than three months.

## 49. §

If the convicted person is sentenced to imprisonment to be executed for an offense committed after the sentencing to community service, and the community service sentence has not yet been executed, the community service or the remaining part thereof shall be converted into imprisonment in such a way that four hours of community service corresponds to one day of imprisonment. The community service remaining after the conversion corresponds to one day of imprisonment.

## 50. §

(1) The fine shall be imposed in such a way that – taking into account the objective gravity of the offense – the number of daily units of the fine must be determined, and – in proportion to the offender's financial, income, personal circumstances and lifestyle – the amount corresponding to one daily unit.

(2) A person who is sentenced to a definite term of imprisonment for an offense committed for the purpose of gain, if they have adequate income or assets, shall also be sentenced to a fine.

(3) * The minimum measure of the fine is thirty daily units, the maximum measure is five hundred and forty daily units. The amount of one daily unit shall be determined at a minimum of one thousand three hundred forints, and a maximum of six hundred and fifty thousand forints.

(4) * The court may, in its decision on the merits – in view of the offender's financial and income circumstances – provide that the offender may pay the fine in monthly installments within a maximum of two years.

## 51. §

(1) If the convicted person does not pay the fine, or in case of authorization of payment in installments fails to pay one monthly installment, the fine or the unpaid part thereof shall be converted into imprisonment.

(2) If the fine was imposed alongside imprisonment to be executed, or the execution of the suspended imprisonment was ordered, the level of execution of the imprisonment replacing the fine shall be governed by the level of the imprisonment. In other cases, the imprisonment replacing the fine shall be executed at the level of prison.

(3) The imprisonment replacing the fine may be shorter than three months.

## 52. § (1) A person may be disqualified from practicing a profession if they commit the criminal offense

a) by violating the rules of their profession requiring professional qualification, or

b) by intentionally using their profession.

(2) Paragraph (1) point a) may also be applied to a person who, at the time of committing the criminal offense, did not practice the activity as their profession, but possesses the professional qualification necessary for practicing the profession whose rules they violated when committing the criminal offense.

(3) * A person who commits a criminal offense against the freedom of sexual life and sexual morality, if they committed the offense against a person who has not reached eighteen years of age, or a person who commits child pornography must be permanently disqualified from practicing any profession or other activity within the framework of which they educate, supervise, care for, or provide medical treatment to a person who has not reached eighteen years of age, or have any other power or influence relationship with such person.

(4) * A person who commits the criminal offense of endangering a minor must be disqualified from practicing any profession or other activity within the framework of which they educate, supervise, care for, or provide medical treatment to a person who has not reached eighteen years of age, or have any other power or influence relationship with such person. In cases deserving special consideration, the mandatory application of disqualification from profession may be dispensed with.

(5) * A person who commits the criminal offense of prohibited influencing of voters' will must be disqualified from being a responsible person in any civil organization and a leading officer in a party. In cases deserving special consideration, the mandatory application of disqualification from profession may be dispensed with.

## 53. §

(1) Disqualification from profession lasts for a definite period or is permanent.

(2) The shortest duration of disqualification for a definite period is one year, the longest duration is ten years. A person who is unfit or unworthy to practice the profession may be permanently disqualified.

(3) * The duration of disqualification from profession begins when the decision on the merits becomes final. If disqualification from profession is imposed alongside imprisonment, the time during which the convicted person serves the imprisonment or evades the execution of the imprisonment shall not be included in its duration. If conditional release is not terminated, the time spent on conditional release must be included in the duration of disqualification from profession.

(4) The court may exempt the disqualified person from permanent disqualification upon request if ten years have elapsed since the disqualification and the disqualified person has become fit to practice the profession, or – if the disqualification was applied due to unworthiness – has become worthy. Even in the latter case, a person who committed the criminal offense in a criminal organization may not be exempted.

## 54. § For the purposes of this subtitle, profession also includes if the offender is

a) a member of the body performing general management of a business organization or its sole executive,

b) a member of the supervisory board of an economic company or cooperative,

c) a member of a sole proprietorship,

d) a sole trader, or

e) a leading officer of a civil organization as defined in the Act on Civil Organizations.

## 55. § (1) A person may be disqualified from driving if they

a) commit the criminal offense by violating the rules of driving requiring a license, or

b) use a vehicle to commit criminal offenses.

(2) A person who commits a criminal offense of driving while intoxicated or driving while under the influence must be disqualified from driving. In cases deserving special consideration, the mandatory application of disqualification from driving may be dispensed with.

(3) Disqualification from driving may also apply to a specific type (air, rail, water or road) and category of vehicle.

## 56. §

(1) Disqualification from driving lasts for a definite period or is permanent.

(2) * The time during which the offender's driver's license – prior to being sentenced to disqualification from driving – was taken away at the scene in connection with the criminal offense, was surrendered to the authority, or the immediate suspension of the driving license was registered in the license registry at the scene, must be included in the duration of disqualification from driving.

(3) The shortest duration of disqualification for a definite period is one month, the longest duration is ten years. Disqualification from driving must be determined in months or years, or in years and months.

(4) * The duration of disqualification from driving begins when the decision on the merits becomes final. If disqualification from driving is imposed alongside imprisonment, the time during which the convicted person serves the imprisonment or evades the execution of the imprisonment shall not be included in its duration. If conditional release is not terminated, the time spent on conditional release must be included in the duration of disqualification from driving.

(5) A person who is unfit to drive may be permanently disqualified. The court may exempt the disqualified person from permanent disqualification upon request if ten years have elapsed since the disqualification and the disqualified person has become fit to drive.

## 57. §

(1) In cases defined in this Act, a person whose presence there would endanger the public interest may be banned from one or more settlements, or from a settlement or a specific part of the country.

(2) The shortest duration of banning is one year, the longest duration is five years.

(3) * The duration of banning begins when the decision on the merits becomes final. If banning is imposed alongside imprisonment, the time during which the convicted person serves the imprisonment or evades the execution of the imprisonment shall not be included in its duration. If conditional release is not terminated, the time spent on conditional release must be included in the duration of banning.

## 58. §

(1) The court may disqualify the offender for a criminal offense committed in connection with a sports event during participation in the sports event, going to or leaving from it

a) from attending any sports event organized in the competition system of any sports association, or

b) from entering any sports facility when it serves as the venue for a sports event organized in the competition system of any sports association.

(2) The shortest duration of disqualification is one year, the longest duration is five years.

(3) * The duration of disqualification begins when the decision on the merits becomes final. If disqualification from attending sports events is imposed alongside imprisonment, the time during which the convicted person serves the imprisonment or evades the execution of the imprisonment shall not be included in its duration. If conditional release is not terminated, the time spent on conditional release must be included in the duration of disqualification from attending sports events.

## 59. §

(1) A non-Hungarian citizen offender whose stay in the country is undesirable must be expelled from the territory of Hungary. The expelled person is obliged to leave the territory of the country and may not return during the period of expulsion.

(2) * A person for whom Hungary provides the right of asylum may not be expelled.

(3) * Expulsion of a person with the right of free movement and residence, as well as long-term residence entitlement on the territory of Hungary, may only take place for committing a criminal offense punishable by imprisonment of five years or more.

(4) Against a person

a) who has been lawfully residing on the territory of Hungary for at least ten years, or

b) * who lawfully resides on the territory of Hungary and whose right to respect for family life would be violated, expulsion may only take place in case of imposing imprisonment of ten years or more, provided that the offender's stay in the country would significantly endanger public security.

## 60. § (1) Expulsion lasts for a definite period or is permanent.

(2) The minimum duration of expulsion for a definite period is one year, the maximum duration is ten years.

(2a) * In cases of illegal crossing of the border barrier (§ 352/A), damaging the border barrier (§ 352/B), obstruction of construction work related to the border barrier (§ 352/C), and human smuggling (§ 353), expulsion imposed alongside imprisonment – with the exception of cases under § 59 (2) and (4) – cannot be waived. In the case of expulsion for a definite period, its duration is twice the duration of the imposed imprisonment, but at least two years. The duration of expulsion may be determined in years, months and days. When applying this paragraph, there is no place for the application of § 50 (2).

(3) A person sentenced to imprisonment of ten years or more may be expelled permanently if – in view of the outstanding gravity of the offence, the nature of its commission, and the offender's connections – their stay in the country would significantly endanger public security. A person with the right of free movement and residence may not be expelled permanently.

(4) * The duration of expulsion begins on the day when the convicted person leaves the territory of Hungary – in the case of a third-country national convicted person who does not have a right of residence in another European Union Member State, the European Union – as specified in the decision on the execution of the expulsion issued on the basis of immigration regulations, or if this date is not known, the deadline designated for this in the decision. If the court sentences an accused person residing abroad or absent to expulsion, the duration of expulsion begins on the day the final decision becomes final.

(5) The court may exempt the expelled person from permanent expulsion upon their request if ten years have elapsed since the expulsion and the expelled person is worthy of it.

## 61. §

(1) A person sentenced to custodial imprisonment for committing an intentional crime must be disqualified from exercising public affairs if they are unworthy to participate in their exercise.

(2) A person disqualified from public affairs

a) does not have the right to vote, may not participate in a referendum and popular initiative,

b) may not be an official,

c) may not be a member of the body or committee of a representative body, may not participate in their work,

d) may not be delegated to the assembly or body of an organization established by an international treaty promulgated by law,

e) may not attain military rank,

f) may not receive domestic decorations and permission to accept foreign decorations,

g) may not be a defender or legal representative in official proceedings,

h) may not hold office in a public body, public foundation, and

i) may not be a leading officer of a civil organization as designated in the law on civil organizations.

(3) * The person disqualified from public affairs loses, upon the final decision becoming final, all memberships, positions, offices, military ranks, mandates and decorations the attainment of which is excluded by paragraph (2), as well as their honorary rank.

## 62. §

(1) Disqualification from public affairs lasts for a definite period, its minimum duration is one year, its maximum duration is ten years.

(2) * The duration of disqualification from public affairs begins when the final decision becomes final. The period during which the convicted person serves the imprisonment or during which they evade the execution of the imprisonment is not included in the duration of disqualification from public affairs. If conditional release is not terminated, the time spent on conditional release must be included in the duration of disqualification from public affairs.

## 63. § (1) Measures

a) reprimand,

b) probation,

c) reparation work,

d) probation supervision,

e) confiscation,

f) confiscation of assets,

g) permanent making inaccessible of electronic data,

h) compulsory treatment,

i) measures under the law on criminal measures applicable to legal persons.

(2) Reprimand, probation and reparation work may be applied independently, in place of punishment.

(3) Probation supervision may be applied alongside punishment or a measure. Probation supervision may not be ordered alongside expulsion.

(4) Confiscation, confiscation of assets and permanent making inaccessible of electronic data may be applied independently, and alongside punishment or a measure.

## 64. §

(1) A person whose conduct at the time of adjudication is no longer dangerous, or is dangerous to society to such a slight degree that imposing the minimum punishment applicable under this Act or applying another measure – not including confiscation, confiscation of assets and permanent making inaccessible of electronic data – is unnecessary, must be given a reprimand.

(2) * With a reprimand, the court or the prosecutor's office expresses its disapproval of the unlawful conduct and calls upon the offender to refrain from committing crimes in the future.

## 65. §

(1) The court may postpone the imposition of punishment for a probationary period for a misdemeanor and a felony punishable by imprisonment not exceeding three years if it can be reasonably assumed that the purpose of the punishment can be achieved by applying a measure.

(2) The following may not be placed on probation:

a) a recidivist,

b) a person who committed the crime in a criminal organization,

c) a person who committed the intentional crime after being sentenced to custodial imprisonment, before completion of the execution, or

d) a person who committed the intentional crime during the probationary period of the suspension of imprisonment.

(3) The probationary period may range from one year to three years; the duration must be determined in years, or in years and months.

(4) The person placed on probation may be placed under probation supervision. If the person placed on probation violates the conduct rules of probation supervision, the probationary period may be extended once, by a maximum of one year.

## 66. § (1) Probation must be terminated and punishment must be imposed if

a) the person placed on probation is convicted during the probationary period of a crime committed before the probation,

b) the person placed on probation is convicted of a crime committed during the probationary period, or

c) the person placed on probation seriously violates the conduct rules of probation supervision.

(2) Except in the cases specified in paragraph (1), upon expiry of the probationary period the offender's criminal liability ceases.

## 67. §

(1) The court may postpone the imposition of punishment for a misdemeanor and a felony punishable by imprisonment not exceeding three years for one year and prescribe the performance of reparation work if it can be reasonably assumed that the purpose of the punishment can thus be achieved. Probation supervision may also be ordered alongside the prescription of reparation work.

(2) Reparation work may not be prescribed for a person who

a) is a recidivist,

b) committed the crime in a criminal organization,

c) committed the intentional crime after being sentenced to custodial imprisonment, before completion of the execution, or

d) committed the intentional crime during the probationary period of the suspension of imprisonment.

(3) * The offender – at their choice – may perform the reparation work at a state or local government maintained institution, a civil organization with public benefit status, an ecclesiastical legal person or for them.

(4) The duration of reparation work must be determined in hours; its minimum amount is twenty-four hours, its maximum amount is one hundred and fifty hours.

## 68. §

(1) If the offender properly certifies the completion of the reparation work within one year, their criminal liability ceases.

(2) * If the offender does not certify the completion of the reparation work or seriously violates the rules of probation supervision, the court shall impose punishment. If the offender cannot certify the completion of the reparation work for health reasons, the deadline for certifying the completion of the reparation work may be extended once, by a maximum of one year.

## 69. § (1) * Probation supervision may be ordered

a) for the duration of conditional prosecutorial suspension,

b) for the duration of parole,

c) for the probationary period of probation,

d) in conjunction with the imposition of reparation work,

e) for the probationary period of suspension of imprisonment if regular monitoring of the offender is necessary for its successful completion.

(2) * The following shall be subject to probation supervision:

a) a person who has been released on parole from life imprisonment,

b) a recidivist who has been released on parole or against whom the execution of imprisonment has been suspended,

c) an offender as defined in Section 38(5) who has been released on parole by application of Section 38(6),

d) for the probationary period of suspension of imprisonment, a person who has been sentenced to imprisonment for a violent criminal offense against a person committed against their relative, or

e) for the probationary period of suspension of imprisonment, a person who has been sentenced to imprisonment for a criminal offense against sexual freedom and sexual morality committed against a person who has not reached their eighteenth year of age.

(3) * For the purposes of application of paragraph (2) point d), relative shall also be understood to include the parent of the offender's child, their former spouse, their former cohabitant, their guardian, their ward, their custodian or their person under custody.

## 70. § (1) * The duration of probation supervision is the same as

a) the duration of parole,

b) the probationary period of probation,

c) the probationary period of suspension of imprisonment,

d) the duration of conditional prosecutorial suspension, but at most five years, or at most fifteen years in the case of parole granted from life imprisonment.

(2) Probation supervision ordered in conjunction with the imposition of reparation work lasts until the person obligated to perform the reparation work proves the completion of the reparation work, but at most for one year.

(3) In the case of Section 69(1), after half of the probation supervision, but at least after one year has elapsed, the probation officer may propose the termination of probation supervision if its necessity no longer exists.

## 71. § (1) The probationer is obligated as a general rule of conduct to

a) comply with the rules of conduct prescribed by law and in the decision,

b) maintain regular contact with the probation officer, and

c) provide the probation officer with the information necessary for supervision.

(2) * The court, or in the case of conditional prosecutorial suspension the prosecutor's office, may prescribe obligations and prohibitions as special rules of conduct in its decision in order to promote the purpose of probation supervision. The court or the prosecutor's office may order that the probationer

a) shall not maintain contact with a specified person who participated in the commission of the criminal offense,

b) * shall keep away from the victim of the criminal offense and, with regard to the victim, from their relative or another person (hereinafter collectively referred to in the application of this section as: affected person), from the residence of the affected person or from other property serving as their habitual residence – including child welfare and child protection institutions –, from their workplace, from institutions or other places regularly visited by the affected person, in particular from healthcare institutions visited for educational, educational-instructional or treatment purposes, from buildings visited during their religious practice,

c) * shall not visit public places and public events of a specified nature, and gatherings, specified public areas,

d) shall not consume alcoholic beverages in public places,

e) shall report at a specified place and at specified intervals, at a specified authority or person,

f) shall establish contact with the state employment authority or shall apply for public employment at the local government,

g) shall pursue specified studies,

h) – with their consent – shall undergo specified medical treatment or healing procedure,

i) shall participate in group sessions organized by the probation officer or in other sessions according to the program of the probation service's community facilitator.

(3) * The court or the prosecutor's office may prescribe other rules of conduct in addition to the rules of conduct listed in paragraph (2), with particular regard to the nature of the criminal offense, the damage caused and increasing the chances of the offender's social integration.

(4) * Against an offender as defined in Section 69(2) points c) and d), the special rule of conduct as defined in paragraph (2) point b) shall be ordered for the protection of the victim or, with regard to the victim, their relative. In the case defined in Section 69(2) point c), the court shall order the application of a technical device tracking the movement of the obligated person to monitor compliance with this rule – except if the technical conditions for its application are not available.

(5) * The court may deviate from the application of paragraph (4) against an offender as defined in Section 69(2) point d) with regard to the circumstances of the commission, the degree of danger to society inherent in the person of the offender, the relationship between the offender and the victim of the criminal offense or another person endangered in connection with the criminal offense, or other sentencing circumstances.

(6) * The ordering of restraint as defined in paragraph (2) point b) does not exclude that the offender – if entitled to contact – may maintain contact with their minor child within the framework of supervised contact.

## 72. § (1) The following object shall be confiscated:

a) which was used as an instrument for the commission of the criminal offense or was intended for that,

b) which came into existence through the commission of a criminal offense,

c) against which the criminal offense was committed, or which was used after the completion of the criminal offense for the purpose of transporting this object,

d) the possession of which endangers public safety or violates legislation.

(2) The press product in which the criminal offense is realized shall be confiscated.

(3) In the case of paragraph (1) points a) and c) – provided that the owner did not have prior knowledge of the commission – confiscation cannot be ordered if the object is not the property of the offender, except if international legal obligation excludes the omission of confiscation.

(4) * Confiscation shall also be ordered if

a) the offender cannot be punished due to juvenile age, pathological mental state or a ground terminating punishability specified in law,

b) * the offender was subjected to reprimand,

c) it cannot be executed during the period of special protection specified in the law on the special protection of loaned cultural property.

(5) Confiscation of an object to which confiscation of assets extends cannot be ordered.

(6) The ownership of the confiscated object passes to the state in the absence of a different provision of law.

(7) There is no place for confiscation after the expiration of the time established for the statute of limitations on the punishability of the act, but at least after five years.

## 73. §

In the cases specified in points a) and c) of paragraph (1) of Section 72, confiscation may be exceptionally omitted if it would constitute an unjustified disadvantage to the perpetrator or owner that is disproportionate to the gravity of the criminal offense, except

a) if that is excluded by an obligation under international law,

b) if the perpetrator committed the criminal offense in a criminal organization,

c) * in the case of drug trafficking, drug possession, facilitating the production of drugs, abuse of substances necessary for drug production, abuse of new psychoactive substances, abuse of performance-enhancing substances, pharmaceutical counterfeiting, counterfeiting of medical products, abuse of poison, abuse of harmful consumer products, environmental damage, damage to nature, cruelty to animals, illegal fishing, poaching, organizing prohibited animal fights, violation of the rules of waste management, abuse of ozone-depleting substances, abuse of radioactive materials, abuse related to the operation of nuclear facilities, abuse of explosives or blasting agents, abuse of firearms or ammunition, abuse of weapons prohibited by international treaty, abuse of military technology products or services, abuse of dual-use products, or violation of obligations related to the keeping of dangerous dogs.

## 74. § (1) Asset confiscation must be ordered on

a) assets derived from the commission of the criminal offense, which the perpetrator obtained during the commission of the criminal offense or in connection with it, b)–c) *

d) assets that replaced assets derived from the commission of the criminal offense, obtained during the commission of the criminal offense or in connection with it,

e) assets that were provided or intended for the purpose of committing the criminal offense, to ensure the conditions necessary for or facilitating it,

f) assets that were the object of the pecuniary advantage given or promised.

(2) Asset confiscation must also be ordered on assets derived from the commission of the criminal offense, obtained during the commission of the criminal offense or in connection with it, with which another person has been enriched. If a business organization has been enriched with such assets, the asset confiscation must be ordered against the business organization.

(3) * If the perpetrator or the person enriched under paragraph (2) has died, or the business organization has been transformed, the asset confiscation must be ordered against the legal successor on the assets under paragraph (1) or Section 74/A. to which the succession occurred.

(5) Asset confiscation may not be ordered

a) on assets that serve as security for a civil claim asserted during the criminal proceedings,

b) on assets that were acquired in good faith, for consideration,

c) * on assets under points a) and d) of paragraph (1) or under paragraph (2), if in connection with them, due to the same facts as the criminal offense that is the subject of the criminal proceedings, an administrative authority, the state tax and customs authority has established a payment obligation in a final decision or the court has established it in a final judgment in administrative litigation, up to the extent of the payment obligation.

74/A. § * (1) Until proven otherwise, assets that must be regarded as subject to asset confiscation, and asset confiscation must be ordered on assets that the perpetrator acquired

a) during his participation in a criminal organization,

b) during the commission of placing drugs on the market or trafficking in them,

c) during the commission of human smuggling committed on a commercial basis or in complicity.

(2) Until proven otherwise, assets that must be regarded as subject to asset confiscation, and asset confiscation must be ordered on assets that

a) placing drugs on the market or trafficking in them [Section 176 paragraphs (1)–(4) and (6), Section 177 paragraph (1) point b), point c) subpoint cb) and paragraphs (2)–(3) and (5)], cultivation, production, importation into, exportation from, or transit through the territory of the country of drugs [Sections 178 paragraphs (1)–(5) and 179], facilitating the production of drugs [Section 182 paragraphs (1)–(4)], placing drug precursors on the market, transporting them, carrying out brokerage activities with them [Section 183 paragraph (1) point a)],

b) production, importation into, exportation from, transit through the territory of the country, placing on the market, or trafficking in new psychoactive substances [Section 184 paragraphs (1)–(3) and (5), Section 184/A paragraph (1) point b) and point c) subpoint cb), paragraphs (2)–(3) and (5), Section 184/B paragraph (1) point a), paragraphs (2)–(5), Section 184/C paragraph (1) point a) subpoint aa), point b) subpoint ba), paragraphs (2)–(5)],

c) * trafficking in human beings and forced labor [Section 192 paragraphs (1)–(7)],

d) * procuring (Section 200), facilitating prostitution (Section 201), living off the earnings of prostitution (Section 202), exploitation of child prostitution [Section 203 paragraphs (1) and (3)–(4)], offering, transferring, making available, producing, placing on the market, trading in, or making available to the general public pornographic recordings depicting persons under eighteen years of age [Section 204 paragraph (1) points b)–c), paragraphs (2)–(4), paragraph (5) point b) and paragraph (6)], involving a person or persons under eighteen years of age in a pornographic performance or organizing such a pornographic performance [Section 204/A paragraph (1) point b) and paragraphs (2)–(5)],

e) child labor (Section 209), unlawful employment of third-country nationals [Section 356 paragraphs (1)–(2)],

f) abuse of personal data committed for the purpose of gaining profit [Section 219 paragraphs (1) and (3)–(4)],

g) bribery, accepting bribery, official bribery, accepting official bribery (Sections 290–294),

h) * terrorist act [Section 314, Section 315 paragraphs (1)–(2), Sections 316 and 316/A], financing of terrorism [Sections 318 and 318/A],

i) participation in a criminal organization [Section 321 paragraph (1)],

j) * human smuggling (Section 353), facilitating illegal stay (Section 354),

k) extortion (Section 367),

l) embezzlement committed for particularly high or particularly significant value, in a criminal organization [Section 372 paragraph (5) point a) and paragraph (6)], fraud committed for particularly high value or causing particularly significant damage, in a criminal organization [Section 373 paragraph (5) point a) and paragraph (6)], economic fraud causing particularly high or particularly significant pecuniary disadvantage, committed in a criminal organization [Section 374 paragraph (5) point a) and paragraph (6)], breach of fiduciary duty causing particularly high or particularly significant pecuniary disadvantage, committed in a criminal organization [Section 376 paragraph (5) point a) and paragraph (6)],

m) fraud committed using an information system [Section 375 paragraphs (1)–(5)],

n) counterfeiting money [Section 389 paragraphs (1)–(3)], facilitating counterfeiting money (Section 390), counterfeiting cash-substitute payment instruments [Section 392 paragraphs (1)–(2)], abuse of cash-substitute payment instruments [Section 393 paragraphs (1)–(2)], facilitating counterfeiting of cash-substitute payment instruments (Section 394),

o) budgetary fraud causing particularly high or particularly significant pecuniary disadvantage, committed in a criminal organization [Section 396 paragraphs (4)–(5)],

p) money laundering (Section 399),

q) unlawful data acquisition committed in complicity or on a commercial basis [Section 422 paragraph (4) points b) and c)], circumventing technical measures ensuring the protection of an information system [Section 424 paragraph (1)] the perpetrator acquired in the five years preceding the initiation of criminal proceedings, if the assets, or the perpetrator's lifestyle is particularly disproportionate to his demonstrable income circumstances, personal circumstances.

(3) Asset confiscation may not be ordered in the cases under paragraphs (1) and (2) if the perpetrator proves that the assets do not derive from a criminal offense.

## 75. § (1) Confiscation of assets must be ordered expressed in monetary terms, *

a) * if the property subject to confiscation can no longer be found,

b) * if the property subject to confiscation cannot be separated from other property, or its separation would cause disproportionate difficulty,

c) in the case specified in Section 74 paragraph (5) point b).

(2) * Confiscation of assets must also be ordered if

a) the perpetrator is not punishable due to childhood, pathological mental state, or a reason terminating punishability defined by law,

b) * the perpetrator was given an admonishment,

c) it cannot be executed during the special protection period defined in the law on the special protection of loaned cultural property.

(3) The confiscated property passes to the state unless the law provides otherwise.

## 76. §

For the purposes of this subsection, property shall also mean its benefits, rights of pecuniary value, claims, and any advantage with value expressible in money.

## 77. §

(1) Data published on an electronic communication network must be made permanently inaccessible

a) the making available or publication of which constitutes a criminal offense,

b) which was used as an instrument for the commission of the criminal offense, or

c) which came into being through the commission of a criminal offense.

(2) The permanent inaccessibility of electronic data must also be ordered if the perpetrator is not punishable due to childhood, pathological mental state, or a reason terminating punishability defined by law, or if the perpetrator was given an admonishment.

## 78. §

(1) Compulsory treatment must be ordered for the perpetrator of a violent criminal act against a person or a criminal act causing public danger if they are not punishable due to the pathological state of their mental functioning, and there is reason to fear that they will commit a similar act, provided that in case of their punishability, a punishment more severe than one year of imprisonment would have to be imposed.

(2) Compulsory treatment must be terminated if its necessity no longer exists.

## 79. §

The purpose of punishment is to prevent, in the interest of protecting society, the commission of criminal offenses by either the perpetrator or others.

## 80. §

(1) Punishment must be imposed within the framework defined by this Act, keeping its purpose in mind, so that it corresponds to the objective gravity of the criminal offense, the degree of guilt, the dangerousness of the perpetrator to society, and other mitigating and aggravating circumstances.

(2) When imposing imprisonment for a definite term, the mid-range of the sentencing range is decisive. The mid-range is half the sum of the lower and upper limits of the sentencing range.

(3) If this Act prescribes, in case of imposing punishment, the increase of the sentencing ranges defined in the Special Part, the calculation defined in paragraph (2) must be performed with regard to the increased sentencing ranges.

(4) If the court imposes imprisonment, it determines the extent of the punishment disregarding the possibility of suspension of execution or conditional release.

## 81. § (1) In case of concurrence of offenses, one punishment must be imposed.

(2) The concurrent punishment must be imposed on the basis of the most severe among the types of punishment or sentencing ranges established for the criminal offenses in concurrence.

(3) If at least two of the criminal offenses in concurrence are punishable by imprisonment for a definite term, the upper limit of the sentencing range increases by half of the highest sentencing range, but may not reach the combined duration of the upper limits of the sentencing ranges established for the individual criminal offenses.

(4) * If the conditions for this exist with regard to the given criminal offense, the sentencing range for the criminal offense in concurrence must be established on the basis of the provisions on special, multiple and violent multiple recidivists, as well as commission in a criminal organization, prior to the application of paragraphs (2) and (3).

(5) An accessory punishment may not exceed the highest measure or duration defined by law, even in case of concurrent punishment.

## 82. §

(1) A punishment more lenient than the sentencing range may be imposed if its minimum measure would be too severe in view of the principles of imposing punishment.

(2) Based on paragraph (1), if the lower limit of the sentencing range is

a) ten years' imprisonment, instead at least five years',

b) five years' imprisonment, instead at least two years',

c) two years' imprisonment, instead at least one year's,

d) one year's imprisonment, instead imprisonment of shorter duration may be imposed.

(3) In the case of point d) of paragraph (2), instead of imprisonment, detention, community service or fine, or these punishments alongside each other may also be imposed.

(4) In case of attempt or aiding and abetting, if the punishment imposable based on paragraph (2) would also be too severe, the punishment may be imposed based on the next point of paragraph (2).

(5) If this Act permits unlimited mitigation, the minimum measure of any type of punishment may also be imposed.

## 83. §

(1) * In proceedings under Chapter XCIX of Act XC of 2017 on Criminal Procedure, in case of approval of a plea agreement (hereinafter: plea agreement), when imposing punishment, the lower limit of the more lenient sentencing ranges defined in Section 82 paragraph (2) must be taken as a basis.

(2) * If the plea agreement is approved in the case of a defendant who has cooperated to a significant extent with the prosecutor's office or the investigating authority by contributing to the proof of the case or another criminal case, the punishment may also be imposed based on the next point of Section 82 paragraph (2).

## 84. § *

## 85. §

(1) The execution of imprisonment not exceeding two years may be suspended for a probationary period if – particularly in view of the personal circumstances of the perpetrator – it can reasonably be assumed that the purpose of the punishment can be achieved without its execution.

(1a) * In case of illegal crossing of the border barrier (Section 352/A of the Criminal Code), damaging the border barrier (Section 352/B of the Criminal Code), and obstruction of construction work related to the border barrier (Section 352/C of the Criminal Code), the execution of imprisonment not exceeding five years may be suspended for a probationary period.

(2) The duration of the probationary period – unless this Act provides otherwise – may range from one year to five years, but may not be shorter than the imposed imprisonment. The probationary period must be determined in years, or in years and months.

(2a) * In case of illegal crossing of the border barrier (Section 352/A of the Criminal Code), damaging the border barrier (Section 352/B of the Criminal Code), and obstruction of construction work related to the border barrier (Section 352/C of the Criminal Code), the duration of the probationary period may range from two years to ten years, but may not be shorter than the duration of the expulsion imposed due to these criminal offenses.

## 86. § (1) The execution of imprisonment may not be suspended against a person who

a) is a multiple recidivist,

b) committed the criminal offense in a criminal organization, or

c) committed the intentional criminal offense before the completion of the execution of imprisonment or during the probationary period of its suspension.

(2) If imprisonment is executed on the perpetrator due to which the execution of the suspended imprisonment cannot be ordered, then the probationary period is extended by the duration of the imprisonment.

(3) The provision according to paragraph (2) must also be applied in case of imprisonment replacing community service and fine.

(4) In the cases of paragraphs (2) and (3), the duration of the probationary period may exceed five years.

(5) If the perpetrator is sentenced to suspended imprisonment for a probationary period multiple times, and the probationary periods of the imprisonments have not yet elapsed, all probationary periods run concurrently.

(6) Simultaneously with the suspension of the execution of imprisonment, the perpetrator may be placed under probation supervision. If the perpetrator is a recidivist, they are under probation supervision.

## 87. § The suspended imprisonment must be executed if

a) during the probationary period it is established that the execution of the imprisonment was suspended despite the exclusionary ground set forth in Section 86(1),

b) the offender is sentenced to imprisonment to be executed for a criminal offense committed during the probationary period, or

c) the offender seriously violates the conduct rules of probation supervision,

d) * the offender expelled pursuant to Section 60(2a) unlawfully returns to the territory of Hungary during the probationary period of the suspended imprisonment.

## 88. §

In the case of suspension of the execution of punishment by pardon, the provision on the execution of suspended punishment shall be applied mutatis mutandis for ordering the execution.

## 89. §
