(1) * With respect to special and multiple recidivists, the upper limit of the penalty range for the new criminal offense shall be increased by one half in case of imprisonment, but may not exceed twenty-five years.

(2) With respect to special and multiple recidivists, the punishment may be mitigated pursuant to Section 82(1) only in cases deserving special consideration.

(3) The more severe legal consequences specified in paragraph (1) shall not apply if the Special Part of this Act provides for commission as a special recidivist as an aggravated case of the criminal offense.

## 90. §

(1) Section 33(4) shall not apply to violent multiple recidivists.

(2) * With respect to violent multiple recidivists, the upper limit of the penalty range for the criminal offense constituting the basis for qualification as a violent multiple recidivist shall be doubled in case of imprisonment. If the upper limit of the penalty range thus increased would exceed twenty years, or if the criminal offense is punishable by life imprisonment under the law, life imprisonment shall be imposed on the offender.

(3) With respect to violent multiple recidivists, the punishment

a) may not be mitigated pursuant to Section 82(1),

b) may be mitigated without limitation – if the General Part of this Act so allows.

## 91. §

(1) * With respect to a person who committed an intentional criminal offense in a criminal organization, the upper limit of the penalty range for the criminal offense shall be doubled, but may not exceed twenty-five years.

(2) With respect to a person who committed the criminal offense in a criminal organization, expulsion may also be imposed.

(3) Where the commission of the criminal offense in a criminal organization is established, the legal consequences provided in this Act for commission of the criminal offense in complicity shall not apply.

## 92. §

(1) * The entire period of pre-trial detention and criminal supervision during which the court prescribed that the accused may not leave a dwelling, other premises, institution, or fenced area belonging thereto without permission shall be credited against the imprisonment, confinement, community service work and fine imposed.

(2) For the purposes of crediting, one day of pre-trial detention shall correspond to one day of imprisonment, one day of confinement, one day-unit of fine, or four hours of community service work, respectively.

(3) * For the purposes of crediting,

a) one day of imprisonment shall correspond to five days in case of the strict prison regime, four days in case of the medium prison regime, three days in case of the light prison regime,

b) one day of confinement shall correspond to two days,

c) four hours of community service work shall correspond to one day,

d) one day-unit of fine shall correspond to one day of time spent in criminal supervision as defined in paragraph (1).

(4) * The period remaining after crediting pursuant to paragraphs (2) and (3) shall be credited as one day of imprisonment. 92/A. § * (1) * The entire duration of confinement and community service work imposed and executed in misdemeanor proceedings, as well as on-the-spot fine and pecuniary fine shall be credited – following the prosecutor's call, the review or retrial proceedings conducted under the Act on Misdemeanors, against the act concerned by the call, the review or the retrial – against the imprisonment, confinement, community service work, fine and placement in a correctional institution imposed in criminal proceedings.

(2) For the purposes of crediting, one day of misdemeanor confinement shall correspond to one day of imprisonment or confinement, four hours of community service work, one day-unit of fine, and one day of placement in a correctional institution, respectively.

(3) * An on-the-spot fine and a misdemeanor pecuniary fine shall correspond to one day of imprisonment or confinement, four hours of community service work, the same amount of fine, or one day of placement in a correctional institution per five thousand forints, respectively.

(4) Community service work imposed in misdemeanor proceedings shall correspond to the same amount of community service work, and furthermore, six hours of community service work imposed in misdemeanor proceedings shall correspond to one day of imprisonment or confinement, one day-unit of fine, and one day of placement in a correctional institution, respectively.

92/B. § * (1) * If the judgment rendered as a result of retrial, review, remedy filed in the interest of legality, or repeated proceedings modifies the decision rendered in the original case, the punishment or measure imposed and executed in the original case shall be credited. If the type of punishment or measure differs, the provisions of paragraph (2) shall apply to crediting.

(2) For the purposes of crediting,

a) one day of imprisonment shall correspond to one day of confinement, four hours of community service work, one day-unit of fine, six hours of reparation work, or one day of placement in a correctional institution,

b) one day of confinement shall correspond to one day of imprisonment, four hours of community service work, one day-unit of fine, six hours of reparation work, or one day of placement in a correctional institution,

c) four hours of community service work shall correspond to one day of imprisonment, one day of confinement, one day-unit of fine, six hours of reparation work, or one day of placement in a correctional institution,

d) one day-unit of fine shall correspond to one day of imprisonment, one day of confinement, four hours of community service work, six hours of reparation work, or one day of placement in a correctional institution,

e) one day of placement in a correctional institution shall correspond to one day of imprisonment, one day of confinement, four hours of community service work, one day-unit of fine, or six hours of reparation work,

f) six hours of reparation work shall correspond to one day of imprisonment, one day of confinement, four hours of community service work, one day-unit of fine, or one day of placement in a correctional institution, respectively.

## 93. §

* (1) * If the offender is sentenced to multiple fixed-term imprisonments, the sentences imposed with final effect shall be combined into an aggregate sentence – as provided by law – if the offender committed all the criminal offenses before the pronouncement of the earliest first-instance adjudicating decision.

(2) Only such imprisonments to be executed may be combined into an aggregate sentence that have not yet been executed at the time of combining into an aggregate sentence, or that are being executed continuously.

(3) If a suspended imprisonment is to be executed subsequently, it shall be considered as imprisonment to be executed for the purposes of combining into an aggregate sentence.

(4) The following may not be combined into an aggregate sentence:

a) a punishment previously combined into an aggregate sentence,

b) imprisonment replacing a fine or community service work.

## 94. §

* The term of the aggregate sentence shall be determined as if a cumulative punishment were being imposed. However, the term of the aggregate sentence must reach the term calculated as the sum of the most severe punishment and one-third of the shorter punishment or punishments, but may not exceed the total term of the punishments.

## 95. §

* (1) In case of combining imprisonment sentences to be executed at different levels into a combined sentence, the strictest level must be determined as the execution level. If the combined sentence is three years or longer, or in case of multiple recidivism two years or longer, the execution level of the combined sentence must be determined taking this into account.

(2) If the execution level to be determined according to paragraph (1) would result in an unfair disadvantage for the convicted person, one level milder may be established.

(3) * If the possibility of conditional release is not excluded, its earliest date must be determined based on the strictest provision. The convicted person may not be conditionally released from the combined sentence if the possibility of conditional release is excluded in case of any of the imprisonment sentences.

(4) * If the imprisonment sentence in connection with which the convicted person was excluded from the possibility of conditional release due to their own fault is subsequently combined into a combined sentence, then the convicted person may not be conditionally released from the combined sentence either.

## 96. §

* (1) If disqualification from occupation, disqualification from driving, expulsion, disqualification from attending sports events or expulsion from the country was imposed alongside imprisonment, and the imprisonment sentences were combined into a combined sentence, then among several disqualifications from occupation, disqualifications from driving, expulsions, disqualifications from attending sports events or expulsions from the country with identical content, the one that is more disadvantageous for the convicted person must be executed.

(2) The accessory penalty of disqualification from public affairs cannot be combined into a combined sentence. Among several accessory penalties of disqualification from public affairs, the one with the longest duration must be executed.

## 97. §

(1) Adverse legal consequences attached to the establishment of criminal liability, imposition of punishment or application of measures may be established by law.

(2) * Data relating to the establishment of criminal liability, the imposed punishment and the applied measure are contained in an authentic official register until the time specified by law; thereafter, no adverse legal consequence may be established against the convicted person due to the conviction. Recidivism and the adverse legal consequences attached to it as defined in this Act must be established pursuant to Section 389 of Act XC of 2017 on Criminal Procedure, based on data obtained from the criminal register at the time closest to the commission of the new offense, even if these data are subsequently deleted.

## 98. §

(1) As a result of rehabilitation – in the absence of a contrary provision of law – the convicted person is relieved from the adverse legal consequences attached to a criminal record.

(2) A rehabilitated person shall be considered to have a clean criminal record and – in the absence of a contrary provision of law – is not obliged to account for a conviction for which they received rehabilitation.

(3) In case of committing a new offense, rehabilitation does not extend to those criminal law consequences that this Act attaches to the previous conviction.

## 99. § The convicted person may receive rehabilitation

a) by operation of this Act,

b) based on a court decision, or

c) by way of pardon.

## 100. § (1) Rehabilitation occurs by operation of this Act

a) * in case of detention, fine and community service on the day the final decision becomes final,

b) * in case of disqualification from occupation, disqualification from driving, expulsion and disqualification from attending sports events on the day the final decision becomes final,

c) in case of expulsion from the country on the day of completion of execution of the punishment or cessation of its enforceability,

d) in case of suspended imprisonment on the day of expiry of the probationary period,

e) in case of imprisonment imposed for negligent misdemeanor on the day of completion of the punishment or cessation of its enforceability,

f) in case of imprisonment not exceeding one year imposed for an intentional offense, three years after completion of the punishment or cessation of its enforceability,

g) in case of imprisonment exceeding one year but not longer than five years imposed for an intentional offense, five years after completion of the punishment or cessation of its enforceability,

h) in case of imprisonment exceeding five years but not longer than ten years imposed for an intentional offense, eight years after completion of the punishment or cessation of its enforceability,

i) in case of imprisonment for a definite period exceeding ten years imposed for an intentional offense, ten years after completion of the punishment or cessation of its enforceability.

(2) In case of paragraph (1) point b), rehabilitation does not extend to

a) in case of disqualification from occupation, the exercise of the occupation,

b) in case of disqualification from driving, the exercise of driving,

c) in case of expulsion, staying in the locality specified in the judgment or in the specified part of the country,

d) in case of disqualification from attending sports events, the convicted person's right to attend sports events.

(3) * In case of paragraph (1) point d), rehabilitation does not occur if the execution of the imprisonment is ordered. In that case, the rehabilitation rules applicable to enforceable imprisonment apply.

## 101. §

* (1) In case of enforceable imprisonment, the court may grant rehabilitation to the convicted person retrospectively upon request if they deserve it, and half of the time specified in Section 100 paragraph (1) points f)–i) has already elapsed since the completion of the imprisonment or cessation of its enforceability.

(2) In case of suspended imprisonment, the court may grant rehabilitation to the convicted person retrospectively upon request if they deserve it, and half of the probationary period, but at least one year, has already elapsed.

(3) * When assessing merit, the lifestyle of the convicted person since the completion of the punishment, or in case of suspended imprisonment since the final decision became final, must be taken into account, as well as whether – if they had the opportunity – they remedied the harm caused by the offense.

(4) In case of suspended imprisonment, retrospective rehabilitation loses its effect if the execution of the suspended imprisonment is ordered.

## 102. §

(1) * In case of suspension of execution of imprisonment, the court may grant preliminary rehabilitation to the convicted person in the final decision if they deserve it.

(2) Preliminary rehabilitation loses its effect if the execution of the suspended imprisonment is ordered.

## 103. §

In case of application of an accessory penalty, the convicted person is not relieved, nor can they be relieved, from the adverse legal consequences attached to the conviction as long as the execution of the accessory penalty has not been completed or its enforceability has not ceased.

## 104. §

(1) The holder of the power of pardon may grant rehabilitation to the convicted person by pardon even when this otherwise has no place according to this Act.

(2) A person granted rehabilitation by pardon shall be considered to have a clean criminal record for the purposes of adverse legal consequences outside criminal law.

## 105. §

(1) A juvenile is a person who has reached their twelfth year of age but not their eighteenth at the time of committing the offense.

(2) The provisions of this Act shall apply to juveniles with the deviations set forth in this chapter.

## 106. §

(1) The purpose of a penalty or measure imposed on a juvenile is primarily to ensure that the juvenile develops in the right direction and becomes a useful member of society; with this in mind, the education and protection of the juvenile must be kept in view when choosing the measure or penalty.

(2) A penalty shall be imposed on a juvenile if the application of a measure is not expedient. Against a person who had not reached the age of fourteen at the time of committing the offense, only a measure may be applied.

(3) A measure involving deprivation of liberty may be applied or a penalty involving deprivation of liberty may be imposed on a juvenile only if the purpose of the measure or penalty cannot be achieved by other means.

## 107. §

If the offender is a juvenile, active repentance may also have place in the case of committing a misdemeanor defined in Section 29(1) or a felony punishable by imprisonment not exceeding five years.

## 108. §

(1) Reformatory education may also be applied as a measure against a juvenile.

(2) Imprisonment, detention or community service may not be imposed in addition to reformatory education.

## 109. §

(1) The shortest term of imprisonment that may be imposed on a juvenile for any offense is one month.

(2) The longest term of imprisonment that may be imposed on a juvenile who had not reached the age of sixteen at the time of committing the offense is

a) in the case of committing an offense punishable by life imprisonment, ten years,

b) in the case of committing an offense punishable by imprisonment exceeding five years, five years.

(3) The longest term of imprisonment that may be imposed on a juvenile who had reached the age of sixteen at the time of committing the offense is

a) in the case of committing an offense punishable by life imprisonment, fifteen years,

b) in the case of committing an offense punishable by imprisonment exceeding ten years, ten years,

c) in the case of committing an offense punishable by imprisonment exceeding five years, five years.

(4) For the purpose of calculating the limitation period for criminal liability and for the provisions relating to recidivists, the time periods specified in paragraphs (2)–(3) shall be applicable.

(5) * Against a juvenile, the possibility of parole may be excluded pursuant to Section 38(4)(e) and (5) if the juvenile had reached the age of sixteen at the time of committing the offense and is sentenced to imprisonment for ten years or more.

## 110. § (1) The enforcement level of imprisonment is juvenile prison if

a) the juvenile is sentenced to imprisonment for two years or longer for a felony,

b) the juvenile sentenced to imprisonment for one year or longer is a recidivist, or

c) the juvenile sentenced to imprisonment for one year or longer was sentenced to reformatory education for an intentional offense within three years preceding the commission of the intentional offense.

(2) Except for the cases specified in paragraph (1), the enforcement level of imprisonment is juvenile detention facility.

## 111. §

The shortest term of detention that may be imposed on a juvenile is three days, the longest term is thirty days.

## 112. §

* Community service may be imposed on a juvenile if they had reached the age of sixteen at the time of the adjudicative decision.

## 113. §

* (1) A fine may be imposed on a juvenile if they have independent earnings, income or adequate property.

(2) * In the case of a juvenile, the minimum amount of a fine is fifteen, the maximum amount is two hundred and fifty day-fines, the amount of one day-fine shall be determined at a minimum of six hundred and fifty, at a maximum of sixty-five thousand forints.

(3) In the case of a juvenile, if the fine is uncollectible, it shall be converted to

a) community service, if Section 112 permits, or

b) imprisonment.

(4) The community service replacing the fine shall be determined so that two hours of community service replace one day-fine. Otherwise, the provisions of Section 47 shall apply to community service replacing a fine, with the proviso that its duration may differ from those specified in Section 47(1).

(5) If the juvenile does not voluntarily fulfill their work obligation, the community service or the remaining part thereof shall be converted to imprisonment. Otherwise, the provisions of Section 48 shall apply.

113/A. § * Against a juvenile, the application of Section 52(3) may be dispensed with in cases deserving special consideration.

## 114. § Expulsion may have place against a juvenile if

a) imprisonment for ten years or more has been imposed on them,

b) their stay in the country would significantly endanger public security, and

c) their right to respect for family life is not violated.

## 115. §

A juvenile may be disqualified from public affairs only in the case of imposing imprisonment exceeding one year.

## 116. §

(1) Probation may have place against a juvenile for any offense.

(2) The probationary period may range from one year to two years.

(3) In the cases specified in Section 66(1), the court shall order reformatory education or impose a penalty.

## 117. §

* Reparation work may be prescribed against a juvenile if they had reached the age of sixteen at the time of the adjudicative decision.

## 118. §

A juvenile with an adequate family environment may not be banned from the settlement where their family lives.

## 119. § (1) * A juvenile is under probation supervision

a) during the period of parole,

b) during the probationary period of probation,

c) during the probationary period of suspension of imprisonment,

d) during the period of temporary release from reformatory institution,

e) during the period of conditional prosecutorial suspension.

(2) Probation supervision shall also be ordered against a juvenile in addition to prescribing reparation work.

(3) * Section 71(4) may not be applied against a juvenile.

## 120. §

(1) * The court shall order reformatory education if the institutional placement of the juvenile is necessary in the interest of their effective education. Reformatory education may not be ordered against a person who had reached the age of twenty at the time of the adjudicative decision.

(2) The term of reformatory education may range from one year to four years.

## 121. §

(1) In the case of ordering reformatory education, the court shall establish that the juvenile may be temporarily released from the institution after serving half of the reformatory education if

a) they have spent at least one year in the institution, and

b) it can reasonably be assumed that the purpose of the measure can be achieved without further reformatory education.

(2) The duration of temporary release is equal to the remaining part of reformatory education, but at least one year.

(3) The court shall terminate the temporary release if the juvenile is sentenced to imprisonment during the temporary release – except for the case specified in Section 122 – or reformatory education is ordered against them. If the court imposes another penalty or applies another measure against the juvenile, the court may terminate the temporary release.

(4) In the case of terminating the temporary release, the time spent on temporary release may not be credited toward the reformatory education.

## 122. §

If the juvenile is sentenced to enforceable imprisonment for an offense committed after the sentencing to reformatory education, during the reformatory education or temporary release, the imprisonment penalty shall be executed. In this case, the remaining part of reformatory education shall be converted to imprisonment so that two days of reformatory education are replaced by one day of imprisonment.

## 123. § (1) In the case of a juvenile, the cumulative and combined sentence may not exceed

a) in the case of § 109 (3) point a) twenty years,

b) in the case of § 109 (2) point a) and (3) point b) fifteen years,

c) in the case of § 109 (2) point b) and (3) point c) seven years and six months of imprisonment.

(2) * The imprisonment that may be imposed on a juvenile even upon application of § 90 (2) may not exceed the extent specified in (1) points a) and b).

(3) In case of the concurrence of reformatory education and imprisonment, the imprisonment shall be executed as a combined sentence. The court may extend its duration by a maximum of one year if this is necessary for achieving the objective defined in § 106. However, the duration of the extension may not reach the remaining part of the reformatory education.

## 124. §

(1) If the court has ordered multiple reformatory educations against a juvenile that have not yet been executed at the time of ordering the unified measure, or that are being continuously executed, the court shall order reformatory education as a unified measure against the juvenile.

(2) The duration of reformatory education ordered as a unified measure shall be determined in such a way that it is not shorter than the longest-duration reformatory education, but does not exceed the combined duration of the ordered reformatory educations or four years.

124/A. § * (1) * If the approval of the agreement takes place in the case of a juvenile who had not reached the sixteenth year of age at the time of committing the offense and who cooperated significantly with the prosecutor or the investigating authority by contributing to the proof of the case or another criminal case, the extent of the imprisonment that may be imposed

a) in case of committing an offense punishable also by life imprisonment eight years,

b) in case of committing an offense punishable by imprisonment exceeding five years three years,

c) in case of committing an offense punishable by imprisonment exceeding three years two years,

d) in case of committing an offense punishable by imprisonment not exceeding three years may not exceed six months.

(2) * If the approval of the agreement takes place in the case of a juvenile who had reached the sixteenth year of age at the time of committing the offense and who cooperated significantly with the prosecutor or the investigating authority by contributing to the proof of the case or another criminal case, the extent of the imprisonment that may be imposed

a) in case of committing an offense punishable also by life imprisonment ten years,

b) in case of committing an offense punishable by imprisonment exceeding ten years eight years,

c) in case of committing an offense punishable by imprisonment exceeding five years three years,

d) in case of committing an offense punishable by imprisonment exceeding three years two years,

e) in case of committing an offense punishable by imprisonment not exceeding three years may not exceed six months.

(3) During the imposition of the punishment

a) in case of concurrence of offenses,

b) against a special, multiple or violent multiple recidivist, and

c) in case of committing an offense in a criminal organization, the sentencing range according to (1) and (2) shall be taken as a basis.

## 125. §

* (1) The total time of pre-trial detention and such criminal supervision during which the court prescribed for the accused that he may not leave a dwelling, other premises, institution or the enclosed place belonging to it without permission shall be credited toward the ordered reformatory education.

(2) For the purpose of crediting, one day of reformatory education corresponds to

a) one day of pre-trial detention and

b) three days of criminal supervision as defined in (1).

(3) The duration remaining after crediting shall be credited as one day of reformatory education.

## 126. § (1) The juvenile convict is released by operation of law

a) * on the day the final decision becomes final if the execution of the imposed imprisonment is suspended,

b) on the day of completion of the sentence or the termination of its enforceability if sentenced to imprisonment not exceeding one year for an intentional offense,

c) upon expiration of three years from the day of completion of the sentence or the termination of its enforceability if sentenced to imprisonment exceeding one year but not longer than five years to be executed for an intentional offense.

(2) After completion of imprisonment exceeding one year imposed for an intentional offense, the court may grant release to the juvenile upon request if he deserves it.

## 127. §

(1) * For the purposes of this Act, a soldier is a professional member of the defense forces, the police, the National Assembly Guard, the prison service organization, the professional disaster management body and the civilian national security services.

(2) The provisions of this Act shall be applied to soldiers with the deviations set out in this chapter.

(3) A military offense can be committed as a perpetrator only by a soldier.

## 128. §

(1) A soldier who commits a military offense is punishable according to Chapter XLV if he commits it

a) against a soldier of an allied armed force, or

b) within the framework of humanitarian activity, peacekeeping or humanitarian operation conducted in a foreign theater of operations, or during other foreign service against a soldier of another state during the joint performance of a service task.

(2) For the purposes of (1) point a), a soldier of an allied armed force also includes a member of the police or civil defense service of the state of the allied armed force, for the purposes of (1) point b), a soldier of another state also includes a member of the police or civil defense service of the other state.

## 129. §

A soldier who commits a military offense is also punishable according to Chapter XLV if he commits it

a) during his service in the allied armed forces,

b) within the framework of humanitarian activity, peacekeeping or humanitarian operation conducted in a foreign theater of operations.

## 130. §

(1) A soldier may not be punished for an act performed on order, unless he knew that by carrying out the order he commits an offense.

(2) For an offense committed on order, the person giving the order is also liable as a perpetrator if the soldier knew that by carrying out the order he commits an offense, otherwise the person giving the order is liable as an indirect perpetrator.

## 131. §

Except for the cases defined in § 66 (1), the perpetrator may not be punished for a military misdemeanor if one year has elapsed since the termination of his service relationship.

## 132. §

(1) If the convict can be retained in service, the court shall order the execution of imprisonment not exceeding one year, or detention imposed for a misdemeanor in a military prison, unless the convict is a recidivist.

(2) If the service relationship of the convict has terminated, the level of execution of the sentence or its remaining part is prison.

## 133. §

§ 132 is applicable to the execution of imprisonment imposed as a combined sentence.

## 134. §

Community service may not be imposed on a soldier during the existence of his service relationship.

## 135. § (1) Against a soldier

a) demotion or

b) termination of service relationship may be imposed in addition to another sentence, or if the upper limit of the sentencing range for the offense is not more severe than three years of imprisonment, also independently.

(2) There is no place for the application of military punishment if the soldier is disqualified from public affairs.

(3) * The punishment of termination of service relationship may not be applied against a person with military obligation.

## 136. § (1) Against a soldier

a) reduction in rank or

b) extension of waiting period may be imposed in addition to a sentence.

(2) There is no place for the application of military accessory punishment in addition to military punishment, or if the soldier is disqualified from public affairs.

## 137. §

(1) * Upon demotion, the soldier loses their rank and honorary rank.

(2) Demotion shall be applied if the perpetrator has become unworthy of the rank.

## 138. §

Termination of the service relationship is warranted if the perpetrator has become unworthy of service.

## 139. §

(1) In the case of reduction in rank, the soldier shall be placed in a rank one level lower than the rank held at the time of adjudication of the offense.

(2) Reduction in rank shall be applied if the offense entails harm to the prestige of the rank, but demotion is not necessary.

(3) Simultaneously with the reduction in rank, the time to be served in the lower rank shall be determined for a period of one to two years.

## 140. §

(1) * In case of extension of the waiting time, the waiting time prescribed for promotion of the military servicemember to the next rank shall be extended. The extension shall be determined in years, and its duration may not exceed half of the waiting time prescribed for the rank.

(1a) * In case of extension of the waiting time, for professional members of the police, the Parliamentary Guard, the penitentiary organization, the professional disaster management authority and the civilian national security services, the waiting time prescribed for advancement to the next pay grade shall be extended. The extension shall be determined in years, and its duration may not exceed half of the waiting time assigned to the pay grade.

(2) * The waiting time shall be extended if the soldier must earn the promotion or advancement through serving a longer waiting time.

(3) * In the case of military servicemembers, the waiting time specified in paragraphs (1) and (2) shall be understood as mandatory waiting time.

## 141. §

* (1) * Discharge occurs by operation of law in the case of demotion and termination of service relationship on the day the decision on the merits becomes final.

(2) * The court may grant the convicted person advance discharge from the disadvantages attached to a criminal record in the decision on the merits if it orders the imprisonment to be executed in a military detention facility. This discharge takes effect on the day the sentence is served or its enforceability ceases.

(3) The application of a military accessory penalty does not prevent the discharge of the convicted person.

## 142. §

(1) Whoever, with the intent to destroy, in whole or in part, a national, ethnic, racial or religious group,

a) kills members of the group,

b) causes serious bodily or mental harm to members of the group,

c) subjects the group to conditions of life threatening it or its individual members with destruction,

d) takes measures aimed at preventing births within the group,

e) transfers children belonging to the group to another group,

shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from ten to twenty years or life imprisonment.

(2) Whoever commits preparation for genocide shall be punishable by imprisonment from two to eight years.

## 143. §

(1) Whoever, as part of a widespread or systematic attack against the civilian population,

a) commits homicide,

b) subjects the population or a part thereof to conditions of life threatening it or its individual members with destruction,

c) displaces the population or a part thereof from their lawful place of residence,

d) * commits human trafficking and forced labor,

e) deprives another of their personal liberty or unlawfully maintains their detention,

f) coerces another into sexual violence or its toleration, prostitution, carrying a fetus to term or abortion,

g) causes serious bodily or mental harm to another,

h) deprives a group defined on the basis of political, national, ethnic, cultural, religious, gender or other criteria, or a member thereof, of fundamental rights on account of their membership in the group,

shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from ten to twenty years or life imprisonment.

(2) Whoever commits preparation for a crime against humanity shall be punishable by imprisonment from two to eight years.

(3) For the purposes of this section, a widespread or systematic attack against the civilian population means any conduct involving the multiple commission of acts described in paragraph (1) against the civilian population, in furtherance of or to promote a state or organizational policy.

## 144. §

(1) Whoever, for the purpose of establishing and maintaining domination by one racial group of persons over any other racial group of persons and systematically oppressing the other racial group,

a) kills members of a racial group or groups,

b) subjects a racial group or groups to living conditions designed to bring about the total or partial physical destruction of the group or groups,

shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from ten to twenty years or life imprisonment.

(2) Whoever commits other crimes of apartheid shall be punishable by imprisonment from five to fifteen years.

(3) The punishment is imprisonment from ten to twenty years or life imprisonment if the other crime of apartheid leads to serious consequences.

(4) Whoever commits preparation for apartheid,

a) in the case specified in paragraph (1) shall be punishable by imprisonment from five to ten years,

b) in the case specified in paragraph (2) shall be punishable by imprisonment from two to eight years.

(5) For the purposes of paragraphs (2)-(3), other crimes of apartheid means the crimes of apartheid defined in points a)/(ii), a)/(iii), c), d), e) and f) of Article II of the International Convention on the Suppression and Punishment of the Crime of Apartheid adopted by the General Assembly of the United Nations in New York on 30 November 1973, promulgated by Decree-Law No. 27 of 1976.

## 145. §

The following shall be equally liable with the perpetrator of an offense defined in this chapter:

a) a military superior or a person actually acting as a military superior (hereinafter referred to together as: superior for the purposes of this section) as well, if a person under their effective subordination and control or effective authority and control committed an offense defined in this chapter, and the superior knew or, under the circumstances, should have known of the commission of this offense or its preparation, and did not take the measures within their authority necessary to prevent the offense, or did not file a complaint without delay after learning of the commission of the offense;

b) an official or foreign official person in a leadership position not falling under point a) (hereinafter for the purposes of this section: official leader) as well, if a person under their effective authority and control (hereinafter for the purposes of this section: subordinate) committed an offense defined in this chapter, as a result of their failure to exercise proper control over them, if ba) they knew that their subordinate committed or was preparing such an offense, or consciously disregarded circumstances clearly indicating this, bb) the offense concerned an activity falling within their effective tasks or competence, and bc) they did not take all necessary and reasonable measures within their authority to prevent its commission, or did not file a complaint without delay after learning of the commission of the offense.

## 146. §

(1) Anyone who, in the territory of Hungary, recruits for military service or other service of military interest into a foreign armed organization – excluding allied armed forces – or mediates contractors for such service, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years.

(2) A Hungarian citizen who – excluding allied armed forces – voluntarily joins, offers to join a foreign armed organization participating in an international or non-international armed conflict, or participates in training in such an armed organization, shall be punishable under subsection (1).

(3) If a person recruited or mediated for military service or other service of military interest into a foreign armed organization in the territory of Hungary has not reached the age of eighteen, the recruiter or mediator shall be punishable by imprisonment from two to eight years.

## 147. §

(1) Anyone who violates the conditions of an armistice shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years.

(2) The penalty shall be imprisonment from five to ten years if the violation of the armistice leads to particularly serious consequences.

## 148. §

(1) Anyone who assaults the enemy's war envoy or their escort, unlawfully detains them, or uses other violence against them, if a more serious crime is not committed, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment up to three years.

(2) Anyone who kills the war envoy or their escort shall be punishable by imprisonment from ten to twenty years or life imprisonment.

## 149. § (1) Anyone who, in time of war,

a) directs an attack against protected persons, or

b) directs an attack that may cause the death or serious injury of protected persons, such that it is manifestly excessive in relation to the anticipated direct military advantage, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from ten to twenty years or life imprisonment.

(2) Anyone who, in time of war, subjects a protected person to

a) killing,

b) forcing them into living conditions that threaten them with destruction,

c) forcing them to sexual violence or to tolerate it, to prostitution, to carry a fetus to term or to abort,

d) unlawfully depriving them of their personal liberty,

e) deporting or removing them from their lawful place of residence, except where justified by important military necessity, shall be punishable by imprisonment from ten to twenty years or life imprisonment.

(3) Anyone who, in time of war, violates the right of a protected person to medical self-determination, or causes serious physical or mental harm to the protected person, shall be punishable by imprisonment from five to fifteen years.

(4) Anyone who, in time of war, deprives a protected person or a group of protected persons of the right to a fair and regular trial, shall be punishable by imprisonment from two to eight years.

(5) Anyone who commits preparation for the crime defined in subsection (1) or (2) shall be punishable by imprisonment from one to five years.

## 150. §

Anyone who, in time of war, gives an order or instruction to their subordinate to leave no survivors, if a more serious crime is not committed, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years.

## 151. §

(1) Anyone who uses a protected person to shield a specific area or military forces from enemy military operations shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from five to ten years.

(2) Anyone who commits preparation for the commission of the crime defined in subsection (1) shall be punishable by imprisonment from one to five years.

## 152. § (1) Anyone who

a) forces a protected person to serve in the armed forces or group of a hostile power,

b) forces a citizen of a hostile power to participate in military operations against their own state, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from five to ten years.

(2) Anyone who commits preparation for the commission of the crime defined in subsection (1) shall be punishable by imprisonment from one to five years.

(3) Anyone who calls up, enlists a person who has not reached the age of eighteen into armed forces or groups, or induces them to participate in war, provides assistance for this, or ensures the necessary conditions for this, shall be punishable by imprisonment from five to fifteen years.

## 153. §

(1) Anyone who, in time of war, launches or continues an attack against an installation that does not qualify as a military objective and is militarily undefended, or launches or continues an attack that causes damage in a militarily undefended installation or may cause widespread, long-term and severe damage to the natural environment which is manifestly excessive in relation to the anticipated direct military advantage, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from two to eight years.

(2) The penalty shall be imprisonment from five to ten years if the attack is directed against

a) a hospital, place for the treatment or accommodation of the sick and wounded, or

b) cultural property protected by international treaty.

(3) Anyone who uses or utilizes cultural property protected by international treaty or their immediate surroundings for military purposes, or appropriates, pillages, damages or destroys such property, shall be punishable under subsection (2).

(4) The penalty shall be imprisonment from five to fifteen years if the crime defined in subsection (2) point b) or subsection (3) is committed against cultural property under special or enhanced protection under international treaty, or their immediate surroundings.

(5) For the purposes of this Section:

a) cultural property: cultural property defined in Article 1 of the International Convention for the Protection of Cultural Property in the Event of Armed Conflict concluded at The Hague on 14 May 1954, promulgated by Decree-Law No. 14 of 1957,

b) cultural property under special protection: cultural property defined in Article 8 of the Convention referred to in point a),

c) cultural property under enhanced protection: cultural property defined in Article 10 of the Second Protocol to the Convention referred to in point a), promulgated by Act XXIX of 2006.

## 154. § (1) Anyone who, in time of war, in an operational or occupied territory *

a) pillages civilian property,

b) deprives the population of property necessary for their subsistence by coercing services or otherwise, or destroys such property, except where justified by important military necessity, if a more serious crime is not committed, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years.

(2) Anyone who pillages the fallen, the wounded or the sick on the battlefield shall be punishable by imprisonment from two to eight years.

## 155. §

(1) * Anyone who, in time of war, in an operational or occupied territory, uses or has used weapons or means of warfare prohibited by international treaty shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from ten to twenty years or life imprisonment.

(2) Anyone who commits preparation for the commission of the crime defined in subsection (1) shall be punishable by imprisonment from one to five years.

## 156. §

Anyone who, in time of war, directs an attack against the personnel, equipment, means, units or vehicles of a humanitarian, relief or peacekeeping organization operating in accordance with the Charter of the United Nations, if they are entitled to the protection afforded to protected persons or installations by the international law of armed conflict, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from five to ten years.

## 157. §

Anyone who, in time of war, misuses the emblem of the red cross, the red crescent or the red crystal, or other emblems or signs serving a similar purpose and protected by international law, if a more serious crime is not committed, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years.

## 158. §

Other war crimes and the penalties imposable for their commission are governed by the provisions of Decree No. 81/1945. (II. 5.) of the Council of Ministers, as amended and supplemented by Decree No. 1440/1945. (V. 1.) of the Council of Ministers, elevated to the force of law by Act VII of 1945.

## 159. § The following shall be held equally liable with the perpetrator of a crime defined in this chapter

a) a military superior or a person actually acting as a military commander (hereinafter referred to jointly as: superior for the purposes of this §), if a crime defined in this chapter was committed by a person under their actual subordination and control, or actual authority and control, and the superior knew or, based on the circumstances, should have known about the commission of this crime or its preparation, and did not take the necessary measures within their competence to prevent the crime, or did not make a report without delay after becoming aware of the commission of the crime;

b) an official person or foreign official person in a leading position not falling under point a) (hereinafter referred to as: official leader for the purposes of this §) as well, if a crime defined in this chapter was committed by a person under their actual authority and control (hereinafter referred to as: subordinate for the purposes of this §) as a result of their failure to exercise proper control over them, if ba) they knew that their subordinate committed or was preparing such a crime, or consciously disregarded circumstances clearly indicating this, bb) the crime concerned activities falling within their actual duties or competence, and bc) they did not take all necessary and reasonable measures within their competence to prevent its commission, or did not make a report without delay after becoming aware of the commission of the crime.

## 160. §

(1) A person who kills another shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from five to fifteen years.

(2) The penalty shall be imprisonment from ten to twenty years or life imprisonment if the homicide is committed

a) with premeditation,

b) from greed for gain,

c) from base motives or for a base purpose,

d) with particular cruelty,

e) against an official person or foreign official person, during or because of their official proceedings, against a person performing public duties during the performance of such duties, or against a person who comes to the support or protection of the official, foreign official or person performing public duties,

f) against multiple persons,

g) endangering the lives of multiple persons,

h) as a special recidivist,

i) against a person who has not reached the age of fourteen,

j) * against a person incapable of self-defense,

k) * against a person with limited capacity to defend against the crime due to their advanced age or disability, or

l) * after charges have been brought against the perpetrator for homicide.

(3) A person who commits preparation for homicide shall be punishable by imprisonment from one to five years.

(4) A person who commits homicide by negligence shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment from one to five years.

(5) A person who incites a person who has not reached the age of fourteen or is incapable of expressing their will to commit suicide shall be punishable pursuant to paragraph (1) if the suicide is committed.

(6) For the purposes of point h) of paragraph (2), crimes of a similar nature for the purposes of special recidivism are

a) genocide [§ 142 paragraph (1) point a)], homicide committed in strong emotion (§ 161),

b) the more serious cases of kidnapping and violence against a superior or service member [§ 190 paragraph (4), § 445 paragraph (5) point a)],

c) the more serious cases of acts of terrorism, hijacking of a vehicle, and mutiny, if committed by intentionally causing death [§ 314 paragraph (1), § 320 paragraph (2), § 442 paragraph (4)].

(7) * Point l) of paragraph (2) may be applied if the perpetrator

a) is convicted of the previously committed homicide in the same proceeding as for the homicide committed after the charges were brought, or

b) has been convicted with final effect for the previously committed homicide.

## 161. §

A person who kills another in strong emotion arising from justifiable cause shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from two to eight years.

## 162. §

(1) A person who incites another to commit suicide or provides assistance for its commission shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years if the suicide is attempted or committed.

(2) A person who has reached the age of eighteen who incites a person who has not reached the age of eighteen to commit suicide or provides assistance for its commission shall be punishable by imprisonment from two to eight years if the suicide is attempted or committed.

## 163. §

(1) A person who performs an abortion on another's fetus shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.

(2) The penalty shall be imprisonment from one to five years if the abortion is committed

a) for profit,

b) without the consent of the pregnant woman, or

c) causing serious bodily injury or endangering life.

(3) The penalty shall be imprisonment from two to eight years if the abortion causes the death of the pregnant woman.

(4) A woman who performs or has performed an abortion on her own fetus shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding one year.

## 164. § (1) A person who violates the bodily integrity or health of another commits bodily harm.

(2) If the injury or illness caused by the bodily harm heals within eight days, the perpetrator shall be punishable for the misdemeanor of minor bodily harm by imprisonment not exceeding two years.

(3) If the injury or illness caused by the bodily harm heals beyond eight days, the perpetrator shall be punishable for the felony of serious bodily harm by imprisonment not exceeding three years.

(4) The penalty shall be imprisonment not exceeding three years for a felony if the minor bodily harm is committed

a) from base motives or for a base purpose,

b) against a person incapable of self-defense or of expressing their will, or

c) against a person with limited capacity to defend against the crime due to their advanced age or disability.

(5) The penalty shall be imprisonment from one to five years if the minor bodily harm causes permanent disability or serious deterioration of health.

(6) The penalty shall be imprisonment from one to five years if the serious bodily harm is committed

a) from base motives or for a base purpose,

b) against a person incapable of self-defense or of expressing their will,

c) against a person with limited capacity to defend against the crime due to their advanced age or disability,

d) causing permanent disability or serious deterioration of health,

e) with particular cruelty.

(7) A person who commits preparation for a crime defined in paragraph (3) or (6) shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding one year.

(8) The penalty shall be imprisonment from two to eight years if the bodily harm causes endangerment of life or death.

(9) A person who commits serious bodily harm by negligence shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by

a) in the case defined in paragraph (3) by imprisonment not exceeding one year,

b) in the cases defined in points b)–c) of paragraph (6) by imprisonment not exceeding three years,

c) in case of causing life-threatening injury by imprisonment from one to five years.

(10) The crime defined in paragraph (2) may be punished only upon private motion.

## 165. §

(1) Whoever, by violating an occupational rule, negligently exposes the life, bodily integrity or health of another or others to direct danger, or causes bodily harm, shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding one year.

(2) The punishment shall be

a) imprisonment for up to three years if the offense causes permanent disability, serious deterioration of health, or a mass disaster,

b) imprisonment from one year to five years if the offense causes death,

c) imprisonment from two years to eight years if the offense causes the death of more than two persons, or causes a fatal mass disaster.

(3) If the perpetrator intentionally causes the direct danger, for a felony in the case defined in paragraph (1) up to three years, in the case defined in paragraph (2) – according to the distinction made there – from one year to five years, from two years to eight years, or from five years to ten years of imprisonment shall be punishable.

(4) * For the purposes of this Section, occupational rule also includes rules relating to the use and handling of functional firearms, explosives and explosive materials.

## 166. §

(1) Whoever fails to provide assistance that can be expected from them to an injured person or a person whose life or bodily integrity is in direct danger, shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding two years.

(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment for up to three years for a felony if the victim dies and their life could have been saved by providing assistance.

(3) The punishment shall be imprisonment for a felony in the case of paragraph (1) up to three years, in the case of paragraph (2) from one year to five years, if the dangerous situation was caused by the perpetrator, or if they are otherwise obliged to provide assistance.

(4) The last clause of paragraph (3) shall not be applicable against a person who is obliged to provide assistance under traffic rules.

## 167. §

Whoever fails to fulfill their care duty toward a person who, due to their condition or old age, is unable to care for themselves, and thereby endangers the life, bodily integrity or health of the person in need of care, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.

## 168. §

(1) Whoever performs an intervention on the human or fetal genome, or on the genome of a human embryo, aimed at altering it, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one year to five years.

(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment from two years to eight years if the intervention under paragraph (1) causes a change in the genome.

## 169. §

(1) Whoever uses germ cells derived from a deceased person or a dead fetus for a special procedure aimed at human reproduction, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one year to five years.

(2) Whoever commits a preparatory act aimed at the prohibited use of human germ cells, shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding two years.

## 170. §

Whoever performs an intervention aimed at selecting the sex of the child to be born, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one year to five years.

## 171. §

Whoever conducts medical research on a human being without authorization or in deviation from the authorization, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one year to five years.

## 172. §

(1) Whoever conducts medical research on human germ cells or human embryos without authorization or in deviation from the authorization, or creates a human embryo for research purposes, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one year to five years.

(2) Whoever

a) transplants a human embryo into an animal organism,

b) fertilizes human and animal germ cells with each other,

c) implants a human embryo on which research has been conducted into a human organism,

d) uses human germ cells used for research for human reproduction,

e) uses non-human germ cells or embryos for human fertilization or embryo implantation,

f) uses a human embryo to create multiple human embryos or animal embryos, shall be punishable by imprisonment from two years to eight years.

(3) Whoever commits a preparatory act aimed at the offense defined in paragraph (2), shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding two years.

(4) For the purposes of this Section, human germ cell shall also include fetal germ cell.

## 173. §

(1) Whoever conducts research on a human embryo aimed at altering the embryo's genome, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one year to five years.

(2) Whoever

a) uses a human embryo to create an individual with characteristics different from or in addition to those established by conception, or

b) separates the cells of a human embryo, shall be punishable by imprisonment from two years to eight years.

(3) Whoever commits a preparatory act aimed at the offense defined in paragraph (2), shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding two years.

## 174. §

(1) Whoever creates genetically identical human individuals during medical research or intervention, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from five years to ten years.

(2) Whoever commits a preparatory act aimed at the offense defined in paragraph (1), shall be punishable by imprisonment not exceeding three years.

## 175. §

(1) Whoever unlawfully acquires human gene, cell, germ cell, embryo, organ, tissue, the body of a deceased person or part thereof, or a dead fetus, places them on the market for pecuniary gain, or trades in them, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.

(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment from one year to five years if the prohibited use of the human body is committed by an employee of a healthcare provider in the course of their occupation.

(3) The punishment shall be imprisonment in the case defined in paragraph (1) from one year to five years, in the case defined in paragraph (2) from two years to eight years, if the prohibited use of the human body is committed

a) against a person under eighteen years of age,

b) as a business, or

c) in a criminal organization.

(4) Whoever commits a preparatory act aimed at the prohibited use of the human body, shall be punishable for a misdemeanor in the case defined in paragraph (1) by imprisonment not exceeding one year, in the case defined in paragraphs (2) and (3) by imprisonment not exceeding two years.

(5) For the purposes of this Section, embryo shall also include an embryo that has been removed from the mother's body, as well as an embryo created or produced during a special procedure aimed at human reproduction but not implanted in the uterus.

## 176. §

(1) Whoever offers, transfers, places on the market, or trades in narcotics, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from two years to eight years.

(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment from five years to ten years if the offense is committed

a) in a criminal organization,

b) as an official or person performing a public duty, using this capacity, or

c) in the premises of the Hungarian Defence Forces, law enforcement agencies, or the National Tax and Customs Administration.

(3) The punishment shall be imprisonment from five years to twenty years or life imprisonment if the offense is committed with respect to a significant quantity of narcotics.

(4) Whoever provides material resources for the commission of the offense defined in paragraphs (1)–(3) shall be punishable under paragraphs (1)–(3).

(5) Whoever offers or transfers a small quantity of narcotics,

a) in the case defined in paragraph (1) shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding two years,

b) in the case defined in paragraph (2) points b)–c) shall be punishable by imprisonment from one year to five years.

(6) Whoever

a) commits a preparatory act aimed at the offense defined in paragraph (1) or (2), shall be punishable by imprisonment not exceeding three years,

b) commits a preparatory act aimed at the offense defined in paragraph (3), shall be punishable by imprisonment from one year to five years.

## 177. § (1) The person who has reached the age of eighteen who

a) offers, hands over narcotics to a person who has not reached the age of eighteen,

b) places narcotics on the market or trades in them using a person who has not reached the age of eighteen,

c) on the premises of a building designated for educational, public education, child welfare or child protection tasks, or in its immediate vicinity, ca) offers, hands over narcotics, cb) places on the market or trades in them, shall be punishable by imprisonment from five to ten years for a felony.

(2) The punishment is imprisonment from five to twenty years or life imprisonment if the criminal offense

a) is committed for a significant quantity of narcotics,

b) in a criminal conspiracy,

c) as an official or person performing a public duty, exploiting this capacity.

(3) Punishable according to subsections (1)–(2) is the person who provides material resources for the commission of the criminal offense defined therein.

(4) Whoever commits the criminal offense defined in subsection (1) points a) or ca) for a minor quantity of narcotics shall be punishable by imprisonment from one to five years, or from two to eight years in case of commission as an official or person performing a public duty, exploiting this capacity.

(5) Whoever commits preparation directed at the commission of the criminal offense defined in subsection (1) or (2) shall be punishable by imprisonment for up to three years.

## 178. §

(1) Whoever cultivates, produces, acquires, possesses, imports into the territory of the country, exports from it, or transports through it narcotics shall be punishable by imprisonment from one to five years for a felony.

(2) The punishment is

a) imprisonment from two to eight years if aa) committed as a business, ab) in a criminal conspiracy, ac) as an official or person performing a public duty, exploiting this capacity,

b) imprisonment from five to ten years if committed for a significant quantity of narcotics,

c) imprisonment from five to fifteen years if the criminal offense is committed for a particularly significant quantity of narcotics.

(3) Punishable according to subsections (1)–(2) is the person who provides material resources for the commission of the criminal offense defined therein.

(4) Whoever commits preparation directed at the commission of the criminal offense defined in subsections (1)–(2) shall be punishable by imprisonment for up to three years for a felony.

(5) If the criminal offense is committed for a minor quantity of narcotics, the punishment is

a) in the case defined in subsection (1), imprisonment for up to two years for a misdemeanor,

b) in the cases defined in subsection (2) points aa) and ac), imprisonment for up to three years.

(6) Whoever consumes narcotics, or acquires or possesses a minor quantity of narcotics for the purpose of consumption, if no more serious criminal offense is realized, shall be punishable by imprisonment for up to two years for a misdemeanor.

## 179. § (1) The person who has reached the age of eighteen who

a) using a person who has not reached the age of eighteen or

b) on the premises of a building designated for educational, public education, child welfare or child protection tasks, or in its immediate vicinity, cultivates, produces, acquires or possesses narcotics, shall be punishable by imprisonment from two to eight years for a felony.

(2) Punishable according to subsection (1) is the person who has reached the age of eighteen who, using a person who has not reached the age of eighteen, imports narcotics into the territory of the country, exports from it, or transports through it.

(3) The punishment is

a) imprisonment from five to ten years if aa) committed in a criminal conspiracy, ab) as a business, ac) as an official or person performing a public duty, exploiting this capacity,

b) imprisonment from five to fifteen years if committed for a significant quantity of narcotics,

c) imprisonment from five to twenty years or life imprisonment if the criminal offense is committed for a particularly significant quantity of narcotics.

(4) Punishable according to subsections (1)–(3) is the person who provides material resources for the commission of the criminal offense defined therein.

(5) Whoever commits preparation directed at the commission of the criminal offense defined in subsections (1)–(3) shall be punishable by imprisonment for up to three years for a felony.

(6) * If the criminal offense is committed for a minor quantity of narcotics, the punishment for a felony is

a) in the cases defined in subsections (1) and (2), imprisonment for up to three years,

b) in the cases defined in subsection (3) points ab) and ac), imprisonment from one to five years.

## 180. §

(1) * The person who cultivates, produces, acquires or possesses a minor quantity of narcotics for own use, or who consumes narcotics, is not punishable if they confess to committing the criminal offense and by the time the first-instance decision is made, proves by document that they received at least six months of continuous treatment for narcotic addiction, other care treating narcotic use, or participated in preventive-educational services.

(2) * Subsection (1) is not applicable if within two years preceding the commission of the criminal offense

a) the proceedings against the perpetrator were suspended in view of the ground terminating punishability defined in subsection (1), or

b) the criminal liability of the perpetrator was established for drug trafficking or drug possession.

(3) * In the cases of Section 178 subsections (1) and (5)–(6), and Section 179 subsections (1)–(2) and (6) – if subsection (1) is not applicable – the punishment may be mitigated without limit if the perpetrator enables the establishment of the identity of the person selling the narcotics by the time of the indictment. This provision is not applicable if the criminal offense is committed in a criminal organization.

## 181. § (1) The person who has reached the age of eighteen who

a) induces or attempts to induce a person who has not reached the age of eighteen to the pathological enjoyment of an intoxicating substance or agent not classified as a narcotic, or

b) attempts to induce a person who has not reached the age of eighteen to consume narcotics, shall be punishable by imprisonment for up to two years for a misdemeanor.

(2) If no more serious criminal offense is realized, punishable according to subsection (1) is the person who has reached the age of eighteen who provides assistance to a person who has not reached the age of eighteen for the pathological enjoyment of an intoxicating substance or agent not classified as a narcotic.

## 182. § (1) Whoever, for the purpose of cultivating or producing narcotics, the necessary material, equipment or apparatus

a) manufactures, acquires, imports into the territory of the country, exports from it, transports through it, hands over,

b) places on the market, or trades in it, if no more serious criminal offense is realized, shall be punishable by imprisonment from one to five years for a felony.

(2) Punishable according to subsection (1) is the person who provides material resources for the commission of the criminal offense.

(3) The punishment is imprisonment from two to eight years if

a) the criminal offense defined in subsection (1) is committed in a criminal conspiracy, or

b) the criminal offense defined in subsection (1) point a) is committed as a business.

(4) Whoever commits preparation directed at the criminal offense defined in subsection (1) or (3) shall be punishable by imprisonment for up to two years for a misdemeanor.

(5) The person who – before their activity facilitating the preparation of narcotics came to the knowledge of the authorities – discloses their act before the authorities, hands over to the authorities the material, equipment or apparatus in their possession, and enables the establishment of the identity of another person participating in facilitating the preparation of narcotics, is not punishable.

## 183. § (1) Aki az Európai Unió jogi aktusában meghatározott kábítószer-prekurzort

a) engedély nélkül vagy az engedély kereteit túllépve tart, forgalomba hoz, szállít, vagy azzal közvetítő tevékenységet végez,

b) úgy szerez meg, hogy evégből valótlan tartalmú nyilatkozatot tesz, bűntett miatt három évig terjedő szabadságvesztéssel büntetendő.

(2) Aki az Európai Unió jogi aktusában meghatározott kábítószer-prekurzor forgalomba hozatalával, szállításával, vagy az azzal végzett közvetítő tevékenységgel összefüggő értesítési kötelezettséget megszegi, vétség miatt két évig terjedő szabadságvesztéssel büntetendő.

(3) E § alkalmazásában

a) az Európai Unió jogi aktusán a kábítószer-prekurzorokról szóló 273/2004/EK rendeletet (e bekezdés alkalmazásában a továbbiakban: 273/2004/EK rendelet, illetve a kábítószer-prekurzoroknak a Közösség és harmadik országok közötti kereskedelme nyomon követésére vonatkozó szabályok megállapításáról szóló 111/2005/EK rendeletet (e bekezdés alkalmazásában a továbbiakban: 111/2005/EK rendelet),

b) forgalomba hozatalon a 273/2004/EK rendelet 2. cikkének c) pontjában meghatározott fogalmat,

c) szállításon a 111/2005/EK rendelet 2. cikkének c) és d) pontjában meghatározott fogalmat,

d) közvetítői tevékenységen a 111/2005/EK rendelet 2. cikkének e) pontjában meghatározott fogalmat,

e) értesítési kötelezettség alatt a 273/2004/EK rendelet 8. cikk (1) bekezdésében, és a 111/2005/EK rendelet 9. cikk (1) bekezdésében meghatározott kötelezettséget kell érteni.

## 184. §

* (1) Aki új pszichoaktív anyagot kínál, átad, forgalomba hoz vagy azzal kereskedik, bűntett miatt egy évtől öt évig terjedő szabadságvesztéssel büntetendő.

(2) A büntetés két évtől nyolc évig terjedő szabadságvesztés, ha a bűncselekményt

a) bűnszövetségben,

b) hivatalos vagy közfeladatot ellátó személyként, e minőséget felhasználva vagy

c) a Magyar Honvédség, a rendvédelmi szervek vagy a Nemzeti Adó- és Vámhivatal létesítményében követik el.

(2a) * A büntetés öt évtől tíz évig terjedő szabadságvesztés, ha a bűncselekményt jelentős mennyiségű új pszichoaktív anyagra követik el.

(3) * Az (1)–(2a) bekezdés szerint büntetendő, aki az ott meghatározott bűncselekmény elkövetéséhez anyagi eszközöket szolgáltat.

(4) Aki csekély mennyiségű új pszichoaktív anyagot kínál vagy átad,

a) az (1) bekezdésben meghatározott esetben vétség miatt egy évig,

b) a (2) bekezdés b)–c) pontjában meghatározott esetben bűntett miatt három évig terjedő szabadságvesztéssel büntetendő.

(5) * Aki az (1)–(2a) bekezdésben meghatározott bűncselekményre irányuló előkészületet követ el, vétség miatt két évig terjedő szabadságvesztéssel büntetendő.

184/A. § * (1) Az a tizennyolcadik életévét betöltött személy, aki

a) tizennyolcadik életévét be nem töltött személynek új pszichoaktív anyagot kínál, átad,

b) tizennyolcadik életévét be nem töltött személy felhasználásával új pszichoaktív anyagot forgalomba hoz vagy azzal kereskedik,

c) oktatási, köznevelési, gyermekjóléti vagy gyermekvédelmi feladatok ellátására rendelt épület területén, illetve annak közvetlen környezetében új pszichoaktív anyagot ca) kínál, átad, cb) forgalomba hoz vagy azzal kereskedik, bűntett miatt két évtől nyolc évig terjedő szabadságvesztéssel büntetendő.

(2) A büntetés öt évtől tíz évig terjedő szabadságvesztés, ha a bűncselekményt

a) bűnszövetségben,

b) hivatalos vagy közfeladatot ellátó személyként, e minőséget felhasználva,

c) * jelentős mennyiségű új pszichoaktív anyagra követik el.

(3) Az (1)–(2) bekezdés szerint büntetendő, aki az ott meghatározott bűncselekmény elkövetéséhez anyagi eszközöket szolgáltat.

(4) Aki az (1) bekezdés a) pontjában vagy c) pont ca) alpontjában meghatározott bűncselekményt csekély mennyiségű új pszichoaktív anyagra követi el, három évig, hivatalos vagy közfeladatot ellátó személyként, e minőséget felhasználva történő elkövetés esetén egy évtől öt évig terjedő szabadságvesztéssel büntetendő.

(5) Aki az (1) vagy a (2) bekezdésben meghatározott bűncselekmény elkövetésére irányuló előkészületet követ el, vétség miatt két évig terjedő szabadságvesztéssel büntetendő.

184/B. § * (1) Aki

a) új pszichoaktív anyagot előállít, az ország területére behoz, onnan kivisz vagy azon átszállít,

b) csekély mennyiséget meghaladó új pszichoaktív anyagot megszerez vagy tart, bűntett miatt három évig terjedő szabadságvesztéssel büntetendő.

(2) A büntetés egy évtől öt évig terjedő szabadságvesztés, ha a bűncselekményt

a) üzletszerűen,

b) bűnszövetségben,

c) hivatalos vagy közfeladatot ellátó személyként, e minőséget felhasználva követik el.

(2a) * A büntetés két évtől nyolc évig terjedő szabadságvesztés, ha a bűncselekményt jelentős mennyiségű új pszichoaktív anyagra követik el.

(2b) * A büntetés öt évtől tíz évig terjedő szabadságvesztés, ha a bűncselekményt különösen jelentős mennyiségű új pszichoaktív anyagra követik el.

(3) * Az (1)–(2b) bekezdés szerint büntetendő, aki az ott meghatározott bűncselekmény elkövetéséhez anyagi eszközöket szolgáltat.

(4) * Aki az (1)–(2b) bekezdésben meghatározott bűncselekmény elkövetésére irányuló előkészületet követ el, vétség miatt két évig terjedő szabadságvesztéssel büntetendő.

(5) Ha a bűncselekményt csekély mennyiségű új pszichoaktív anyagra követik el, a büntetés

a) az (1) bekezdés a) pontjában meghatározott esetben vétség miatt egy évig,

b) a (2) bekezdés a) és c) pontjában meghatározott esetben vétség miatt két évig terjedő szabadságvesztés.

184/C. § * (1) Az a tizennyolcadik életévét betöltött személy, aki

a) tizennyolcadik életévét be nem töltött személy felhasználásával aa) új pszichoaktív anyagot előállít, az ország területére behoz, onnan kivisz vagy azon átszállít, ab) csekély mennyiséget meghaladó új pszichoaktív anyagot megszerez vagy tart,

b) oktatási, köznevelési, gyermekjóléti vagy gyermekvédelmi feladatok ellátására rendelt épület területén, illetve annak közvetlen környezetében ba) új pszichoaktív anyagot előállít, bb) csekély mennyiséget meghaladó új pszichoaktív anyagot megszerez vagy tart, bűntett miatt egy évtől öt évig terjedő szabadságvesztéssel büntetendő.

(2) A büntetés két évtől nyolc évig terjedő szabadságvesztés, ha a bűncselekményt

a) bűnszövetségben,

b) üzletszerűen,

c) hivatalos vagy közfeladatot ellátó személyként, e minőséget felhasználva követik el.

(2a) * A büntetés öt évtől tíz évig terjedő szabadságvesztés, ha a bűncselekményt jelentős mennyiségű új pszichoaktív anyagra követik el.

(2b) * A büntetés öt évtől tizenöt évig terjedő szabadságvesztés, ha a bűncselekményt különösen jelentős mennyiségű új pszichoaktív anyagra követik el.

(3) * Az (1)–(2b) bekezdés szerint büntetendő, aki az ott meghatározott bűncselekmény elkövetéséhez anyagi eszközöket szolgáltat.

(4) * Aki az (1)–(2b) bekezdésben meghatározott bűncselekmény elkövetésére irányuló előkészületet követ el, vétség miatt két évig terjedő szabadságvesztéssel büntetendő.

(5) Ha a bűncselekményt csekély mennyiségű új pszichoaktív anyagra követik el, a büntetés

a) az (1) bekezdés a) pont aa) alpontjában és b) pont ba) alpontjában meghatározott esetben vétség miatt két évig,

b) a (2) bekezdés b) és c) pontjában meghatározott esetben bűntett miatt három évig terjedő szabadságvesztés.

184/D. § *

## 185. § (1) Whoever, for the purpose of enhancing sports performance, with regard to a prohibited performance-enhancing substance

a) produces,

b) offers, transfers, places on the market,

c) prescribes on a medical prescription or veterinary prescription, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.

(2) The punishment is imprisonment from one year to five years if the offense is committed as a business or in a criminal association.

(3) The punishment is imprisonment from two years to eight years if

a) as a result of the offense, a person who has not reached eighteen years of age obtains a prohibited performance-enhancing substance,

b) a person who has reached eighteen years of age commits the offense defined in paragraph (1) point a) or b) by using a person who has not reached eighteen years of age.

(4) Whoever commits preparatory acts aimed at the abuse of performance-enhancing substances defined in paragraphs (1)-(3) shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding one year.

(5) A person who has reached eighteen years of age who induces a person who has not reached eighteen years of age to use a prohibited performance-enhancing substance, or provides assistance to such person in using a prohibited performance-enhancing substance, shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding one year.

(6) * For the purposes of this Section, prohibited performance-enhancing substance means: any substance which, in view of its active ingredient, belongs to anabolic agents, peptide hormones, growth factors and their related compounds, hormone antagonists and modulators, and is specifically listed in the government decree promulgating the International Convention against Doping in Sport or its amendment.

185/A. § * (1) Whoever

a) falsifies a medicine or veterinary medicinal product, or produces a false medicine or veterinary medicinal product,

b) places on the market or trades in a false, falsified or medicine or veterinary medicinal product not authorized in Hungary, or offers or transfers it in an unjustified quantity,

c) brings into the territory of the country, takes out of it, transports through it a false or falsified medicine or veterinary medicinal product, or acquires or keeps it in an unjustified quantity,
