* (1) Whoever unlawfully enters an information system by breaching or circumventing the technical measures ensuring the protection of the information system, or remains therein exceeding or breaching the scope of their access authorization, shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding two years.

(2) Whoever

a) unlawfully or in breach of the scope of their authorization obstructs the operation of an information system, or

b) unlawfully or in breach of the scope of their authorization modifies, deletes or makes inaccessible data in an information system, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.

(3) * The punishment is imprisonment from one to five years for a felony if the criminal offense specified in subsection (2) affects a significant number of information systems or causes significant harm to interests.

(4) The punishment is imprisonment from two to eight years if the criminal offense is committed against a public utility.

(5) For the purposes of this Section, data means: the representation in any form of facts, information or concepts stored, processed, handled or transmitted in an information system that is suitable for processing by an information system, including a program that ensures the execution of a function by the information system.

## 424. §

(1) * Whoever, for the purpose of committing a criminal offense specified in Section 375, in point d) of subsection (1) of Section 422, or in Section 423, and necessary for or facilitating this

a) produces, transfers, makes accessible, obtains, or puts into circulation a password or computer program, or

b) makes available to another person their economic, technical, organizational knowledge relating to the production of passwords or computer programs, shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding two years.

(2) * The perpetrator of the criminal offense specified in point a) of subsection (1) shall not be punishable if – before the production of the password or computer program necessary for or facilitating the commission of the criminal offense came to the knowledge of the authority acting in criminal matters – they disclose their activity to the authority, hand over the produced object to the authority, and enable the establishment of the identity of other persons participating in the production.

(3) For the purposes of this Section, password means: any identifier consisting of numbers, letters, symbols, biometric data or their combination, enabling entry into an information system or a part thereof.

## 425. §

(1) A person liable for military service who fails to fulfill their obligation to report for military call-up shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years.

(2) Whoever commits the criminal offense by negligence shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding three years.

## 426. §

A person liable for military service who, for the purpose of evading military service,

a) fails to fulfill their obligation to appear or report for call-up, or

b) mutilates their body, damages their health, or engages in deceptive conduct, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from five to ten years.

## 427. §

A person liable for military service who refuses to perform their military service shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from five to fifteen years.

## 428. §

(1) Whoever commits an act aimed at preventing a person liable for military service from fulfilling their call-up obligation shall be punishable for a felony according to the provisions of Section 425.

(2) * Whoever commits an act aimed at

a) withdrawing a person liable for military service in the manner specified in point a) of Section 426, or

b) withdrawing a person liable for military service from military service in the manner specified in point b) of Section 426, shall be punishable according to the provisions specified therein.

## 429. §

(1) * Whoever fails to fulfill their civil defense obligation shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.

(2) The punishment is imprisonment from one to five years if the criminal offense causes serious danger.

(3) Whoever commits the criminal offense specified in subsection (2) by negligence shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding two years.

## 430. §

A person obliged to perform defense work who seriously violates this obligation by absence or in another manner shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.

## 431. §

* Whoever seriously violates or evades their defense and security obligation consisting of economic and material services shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years.

## 432. §

The punishment may be reduced without limitation if the perpetrator of a criminal offense specified in this Chapter voluntarily fulfills the obligation they failed to fulfill.

## 433. § (1) * The provisions of this Chapter shall be applied in a state of war.

(1a) * Point b) of Section 426 and point b) of subsection (2) of Section 428 shall also be applied during the period following the Government's initiation of a state of war.

(2) * Violation of civil defense obligation may also be committed in a state of emergency and state of danger, in addition to what is specified in subsection (1), and violation of service obligation may also be committed in a state of danger.

(3) * Violation of the defense work obligation may only be committed in a state of war.

## 434. §

(1) Whoever, for the purpose of permanently exempting himself from the performance of military service, arbitrarily leaves or stays away from his place of service, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.

(2) The punishment is imprisonment from one to five years if the desertion is committed

a) while armed,

b) in a group,

c) during the performance of important service or by using the service,

d) by applying violence against a person, or

e) to a foreign country.

(3) * Whoever deserts to a foreign country in the manner specified in points a)–c) of paragraph (2), or whoever commits the desertion in time of war, shall be punishable by imprisonment from five to fifteen years.

(4) Whoever commits preparatory acts directed at desertion as specified in paragraph (2) shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding one year, or in the case specified in paragraph (3) for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years.

(5) The punishment of the perpetrator of desertion may be reduced without limitation if he voluntarily reports to the authorities within thirty days. The day of desertion is not included in the time limit.

## 435. §

(1) Whoever absents himself from his place of service arbitrarily or stays away from it, and his absence exceeds two days, shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding one year.

(2) If the duration of unauthorized absence exceeds nine days, the punishment is imprisonment not exceeding three years for a felony.

(3) * If the unauthorized absence is committed in time of war, during humanitarian activities or peacekeeping performed in a foreign theater of operations, the punishment is

a) in the case specified in paragraph (1), imprisonment from one to five years for a felony,

b) in the case specified in paragraph (2), imprisonment from two to eight years.

## 436. §

(1) Whoever, for the purpose of permanently exempting himself from the performance of military service, mutilates his body, causes harm to his health, or engages in deceptive conduct, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.

(2) Whoever commits the offense specified in paragraph (1) in order to temporarily exempt himself from the performance of military service shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding one year.

(3) If the duration of the temporary exemption exceeds six days, the punishment is imprisonment not exceeding two years.

(4) * If the offense is committed in time of war, the punishment is

a) in the case specified in paragraph (1), imprisonment from five to fifteen years,

b) in the case specified in paragraph (2), imprisonment from one to five years for a felony,

c) in the case specified in paragraph (3), imprisonment from two to eight years.

## 437. §

(1) Whoever expressly refuses to perform military service shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.

(1a) * The punishment is imprisonment from one to five years if the refusal of service is committed during a state of defense crisis.

(2) * The punishment is imprisonment from five to fifteen years if the refusal of service is committed in time of war.

## 438. §

(1) Whoever, while on guard, watch, or other standby duty – in violation of the service instructions applicable to the given service – falls asleep, or during the duty consumes alcoholic beverages, or narcotics or substances or preparations with narcotic effects not classified as narcotics, leaves his assigned post, or seriously violates provisions relating to the performance of the duty in any other manner, shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding one year.

(2) If the offense entails a risk of significant detriment to the service, the punishment for a felony is

a) in the case specified in paragraph (1), imprisonment not exceeding three years,

b) * in time of war, imprisonment from one to five years.

(3) The punishment is imprisonment from five to fifteen years for a felony if the offense is committed in a combat situation, or during humanitarian activities or peacekeeping performed in a foreign theater of operations, and results in particularly great detriment.

(4) Whoever commits the offense through negligence shall be punishable for a misdemeanor in the case specified in paragraph (2), according to the distinction made therein, by confinement or imprisonment not exceeding one year, respectively, and in the case of paragraph (3) by imprisonment not exceeding three years.

## 439. §

(1) Whoever exempts himself from an important service task by deception or absence, or renders himself incapable of performing it, commits a misdemeanor and shall be punishable by imprisonment not exceeding one year.

(2) If the offense specified in paragraph (1) entails a risk of significant detriment to the service and is committed during humanitarian activities or peacekeeping performed in a foreign theater of operations, the punishment is imprisonment not exceeding three years for a felony.

(3) * If the offense specified in paragraph (2) is committed in time of war, the punishment is imprisonment from one to five years.

## 440. §

(1) Whoever, in an important service matter, fails to make a report in due time or makes a false report, if the offense entails a risk of significant detriment to the service, shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding two years.

(2) * The punishment is imprisonment from one to five years for a felony if the offense specified in paragraph (1) is committed in time of war, during humanitarian activities or peacekeeping performed in a foreign theater of operations.

## 441. §

(1) Whoever, in order to cause unlawful detriment or to obtain unlawful advantage, abuses his service authority or position, if a more serious offense is not committed, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.

(2) The punishment is imprisonment from one to five years for a felony if the offense results in significant detriment.

## 442. §

(1) Whoever participates in collective, open resistance directed against service order and discipline, which significantly disrupts the performance of service tasks, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years.

(2) The following shall be punishable by imprisonment from two to eight years for mutiny:

a) the initiator, organizer, or leader,

b) a participant who applies violence against a superior or against someone acting against the mutiny.

(3) The punishment is imprisonment from five to fifteen years if the mutiny results in particularly serious consequences.

(4) The punishment is imprisonment from five to twenty years or life imprisonment if the mutiny causes death.

(5) * The punishment is

a) in the case specified in paragraph (1), imprisonment from two to eight years,

b) in the case specified in paragraph (2), imprisonment from five to fifteen years, if the mutiny is committed in time of war.

(6) If the mutiny specified in paragraphs (1)–(2) is committed in a combat situation, the punishment is imprisonment from five to fifteen years.

(7) * Whoever commits preparatory acts directed at mutiny shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years, or in time of war or in a combat situation by imprisonment from one to five years.

(8) In the case specified in paragraph (1), the punishment may be reduced without limitation for someone who ceases the mutiny before it results in more serious consequences, or after being called upon to do so.

## 443. §

(1) Whoever fails to prevent mutiny or its preparatory acts that come to his knowledge to the extent possible, or fails to report it without delay, shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding two years.

(2) * The punishment is imprisonment from one to five years for a felony if the offense specified in paragraph (1) is committed in time of war or in a combat situation.

## 444. § (1) Whoever does not comply with an order shall be punishable by detention for a misdemeanor.

(2) If disobedience to an order is committed in a group, the punishment is imprisonment up to two years.

(3) The punishment is imprisonment up to three years for a felony if the disobedience to the order

a) occurs in the presence of other subordinates or otherwise publicly, either through the express refusal to execute the order or in another offensive manner,

b) entails the risk of significant detriment to the service or to discipline.

(4) * The punishment is imprisonment from one year to five years if the crime specified in paragraph (3) is committed during wartime.

(5) Whoever does not comply with a combat order in war, or with an order concerning the use of weapons during humanitarian activities or peacekeeping performed in a foreign theatre of operations, shall be punishable by imprisonment from five years to fifteen years.

## 445. § (1) Whoever

a) against a superior,

b) against a higher-ranking officer, a guard or other service agent during or because of the performance of their service uses violence, threatens with it, or displays active resistance, shall be punishable by imprisonment up to three years for a felony.

(2) The punishment is imprisonment from one year to five years if

a) the crime is committed while armed, arming oneself, or in a group,

b) the crime also constitutes disobedience to an order,

c) the crime entails serious bodily harm, or the risk of significant detriment to the service or to discipline.

(3) The punishment is imprisonment from two years to eight years if the crime causes permanent disability, serious deterioration of health, or danger to life.

(4) The punishment is imprisonment from five years to fifteen years if the crime causes the death of the victim.

(5) The punishment is imprisonment from ten years to twenty years or life imprisonment if

a) the crime also constitutes intentional homicide, or

b) the crime is committed in a combat situation.

(6) * The punishment is

a) in the case specified in paragraph (1), imprisonment from one year to five years,

b) in the case specified in paragraph (2), imprisonment from two years to eight years,

c) in the case specified in paragraph (3), imprisonment from five years to fifteen years, if the crime is committed during wartime.

## 446. §

Shall be punishable pursuant to § 445 whoever commits the crime specified therein against a person who comes to the defense of or is assigned to protect a superior or service agent.

## 447. § (1) Whoever

a) offends the authority of a superior,

b) offends the authority of a higher-ranking officer performing their service, a guard or other service agent in front of others or in a conspicuously crude manner, shall be punishable by imprisonment up to one year for a misdemeanor.

(2) The punishment is imprisonment up to three years for a felony if the crime is committed in front of multiple soldiers or otherwise publicly.

## 448. §

(1) Whoever incites discontent among soldiers towards the superior, the order, the service order or discipline shall be punishable by imprisonment up to one year for a misdemeanor.

(2) The punishment is imprisonment up to three years for a felony if the incitement results in significant detriment to the service or to discipline.

## 449. §

(1) Whoever offends their subordinate in their human dignity in front of others or in a conspicuously crude manner shall be punishable by imprisonment up to one year for a misdemeanor.

(2) The punishment is imprisonment up to three years for a felony if the crime is committed

a) for base motives,

b) causing severe physical or mental suffering,

c) in front of multiple soldiers,

d) to the detriment of multiple subordinates.

(3) The punishment is imprisonment from one year to five years if the crime causes serious bodily harm or significant detriment to the service.

## 450. § Whoever, abusing their superior authority, with regard to their subordinate

a) subjects them to disciplinary punishment,

b) restricts them in the exercise of their right to complaint,

c) shortchanges them in their dues or financially burdens them,

d) uses them for private purposes,

e) subjects them to more favorable or less favorable treatment compared to others, shall be punishable by imprisonment up to one year for a misdemeanor.

## 451. §

(1) Whoever breaches their superior duty by failing to take the necessary measures for the material support of their subordinate, for their protection from or rescue from imminent danger, if a more serious crime is not committed, shall be punishable by imprisonment up to one year for a misdemeanor.

(2) If the crime entails significant detriment to the service or to discipline, the punishment is for a felony

a) in the case specified in paragraph (1), imprisonment from one year to five years,

b) * during wartime, imprisonment from two years to eight years.

(3) Whoever commits the crime specified in paragraph (2) through negligence shall be punishable by imprisonment up to one year or up to three years for a misdemeanor – according to the distinction made therein.

## 452. § (1) Whoever, in breach of their superior duty,

a) fails to take the necessary measures to prevent or to hold accountable a subordinate's breach of duty or crime,

b) fails to take the necessary measures to overcome a disturbance threatening the service order, discipline or public safety, if a crime against humanity or war crime is not committed, shall be punishable by imprisonment up to one year for a misdemeanor.

(2) If the crime entails significant detriment to the service, to discipline or to public safety, the punishment is for a felony

a) in the case specified in paragraph (1), imprisonment from one year to five years,

b) * during wartime, imprisonment from two years to eight years.

(3) Whoever commits the crime specified in paragraph (2) through negligence shall be punishable by imprisonment up to one year or up to three years for a misdemeanor – according to the distinction made therein.

## 453. §

(1) Whoever breaches their superior duty by failing to supervise their subordinate in the performance of their service, and this entails significant detriment to the service or to discipline, shall be punishable by imprisonment up to one year for a misdemeanor.

(2) If the crime entails particularly great detriment to the service or to discipline, the punishment is for a felony

a) in the case specified in paragraph (1), imprisonment from one year to five years,

b) * during wartime, imprisonment from two years to eight years.

(3) Whoever commits the crime specified in paragraph (2) through negligence shall be punishable by imprisonment up to one year or up to three years for a misdemeanor – according to the distinction made therein.

## 454. § (1) Whoever directly endangers the increase of the unit's readiness by, in breach of their service duty,

a) failing to provide for or protect the stock of necessary armament, combat equipment or other war material,

b) destroying, rendering unusable, or otherwise withdrawing from its intended purpose important armament, combat equipment or other important war material, commits a felony and shall be punishable by imprisonment up to three years, during wartime by imprisonment from two years to eight years.

(2) * If the crime entails particularly great detriment to the service, the punishment is imprisonment from one year to five years, during wartime imprisonment from five years to fifteen years.

(3) Whoever commits the crime through negligence shall be punishable by imprisonment up to one year or up to three years for a misdemeanor in the case of paragraph (1) – according to the distinction made therein, and by imprisonment up to two years or from one year to five years in the case of paragraph (2) – according to the distinction made therein.

## 455. § Whoever, in a combat situation, in breach of their command duty,

a) surrenders the subordinate soldiers to the enemy or allows them to be captured,

b) destroys without compelling necessity the important combat position, installation, combat equipment or other war material entrusted to them, or hands it over to the enemy in usable condition,

c) does not exert against the enemy the resistance of which they are capable, shall be punishable by imprisonment from five years to fifteen years for a felony.

## 456. § Whoever from the performance of combat duty

a) by voluntarily leaving his/her post, hiding or fleeing,

b) by intentionally causing incapacity to fight or by deceptive conduct,

c) by abandoning, damaging or failing to use his/her combat equipment,

d) by voluntary surrender to the enemy, or

e) withdraws himself/herself by other serious breach of service duty, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from five to fifteen years.

## 457. §

(1) * Whoever in time of war incites discontent or causes demoralization among soldiers shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years.

(2) The punishment is imprisonment from five to fifteen years if the criminal offense

a) triggers discontent or other breach of duty of soldiers in a combat situation, or

b) results in other significant detriment to the service.

## 458. § For the purposes of this chapter

a) military service means the service performed by the persons specified in Section 127 (1),

b) combat situation means the conduct of actual combat operations.

## 459. § (1) For the purposes of this Act

1. * criminal organization: a group consisting of at least three persons, organized hierarchically for a longer period, operating conspiratorially, the purpose of which is to commit intentional criminal offenses punishable by imprisonment of five years or more;

2. criminal association is established when two or more persons commit criminal offenses in an organized manner, or agree to do so, and attempt to commit at least one criminal offense, but a criminal organization is not established;

3. a criminal offense is committed by a group if at least three persons participate in its commission;

4. violent conduct shall also include aggressive physical action exerted on another person, even if it is not capable of causing bodily injury;

5. a person commits a criminal offense while armed if they

a) * have in their possession a functional firearm,

b) explosive material,

c) explosive device,

d) an apparatus designed to use explosive material or explosive device, or commits the criminal offense by threatening with an imitation of those specified in points a) to d);

6. a person commits a criminal offense in an armed state if they have in their possession an instrument capable of taking life in order to overcome or prevent resistance;

7. threat: unless otherwise provided, the prospect of serious detriment which is capable of causing serious fear in the person threatened;

8. * business organization: in addition to the business organization as defined in the Code of Civil Procedure, also an organization to whose civil law relations in connection with its business activity the provisions applicable to business organizations must be applied pursuant to the Code of Civil Procedure;

9. * authority includes the court and the public prosecutor's office;

10. war:

- a) the situations defined in common Articles 2 and 3 of the Geneva Conventions of 12 August 1949 for the protection of the victims of war, and in Article 1(4) of Additional Protocol I to these Conventions,
- b) the situations defined in Article 1 of Additional Protocol II to the Conventions referred to in point a),
- c) * state of war,
- d) state of emergency,
- e) in the case of war crimes and military offenses, also the foreign deployment of the Hungarian Defence Forces;
  - 11. official person:
- a) the President of the Republic,
- b) * Member of Parliament, nationality spokesperson and Member of the European Parliament elected in Hungary,
- c) Constitutional Court judge,
- d) * the Prime Minister, the minister, the secretary of state, the administrative secretary of state, the deputy secretary of state and the lord-lieutenant,
- e) the judge, the prosecutor and the arbitrator,
- f) the Commissioner for Fundamental Rights and their deputy,
- g) the notary public and the deputy notary public,
- h) the independent judicial bailiff, the deputy independent judicial bailiff and the candidate independent judicial bailiff authorized to effect service for enforcement purposes,
- i) member of the local government and nationality self-government representative body,
- j) * the commander of the Hungarian Defence Forces with jurisdictional competence, and the commander of a watercraft or aircraft, if authorized to apply the provisions on the investigating authority,
- k) * person performing public authority tasks or providing service at the Constitutional Court, the Sándor Palace, the Office of the National Assembly, the Office of the Commissioner for Fundamental Rights, the Hungarian National Bank, the State Audit Office, a court, public prosecutor's office, central state administrative body, the Guard of the National Assembly, a Budapest or county government office, a local government administrative body or a public body, whose activity forms part of the proper functioning of the body,
- l) * member of an electoral commission;
  - 12. person performing a public duty:
- a) * member of the Defence Forces performing service duties,
- b) person assigned to a civil defence organization and performing civil defence service,
- c) civilian guard in the performance of their activities as defined in the Act on civilian guards and the rules of civilian guard activities,
- d) * ecclesiastical person and member of a religious association who professionally performs religious ceremonies,
- e) the defence counsel, the legal representative, the expert, and the service effector who is not an official person in court or other administrative proceedings,
- f) * healthcare professional, other person in an employment relationship with a healthcare provider and patient rights representative in the cases specified in the Act on healthcare,
- g) member of the state ambulance service and other organization authorized to provide rescue services in connection with rescue and patient transport,
- h) * member of the municipal and facility fire service, and the voluntary fire association in the performance of their firefighting and technical rescue duties,
- i) * in cases specified in the Act on national public education, the teacher, and the employee directly supporting educational and teaching work, in cases specified in the Act on vocational training, the instructor, and in cases specified in the Act on national higher education, the instructor, teacher and researcher of the higher education institution,
- j) person employed in a position specified in the Act on the protection of children and guardianship administration, and in the Act on social administration and social benefits, in the performance of this activity,
- k) member of the forestry professional staff and authorized forestry professional staff within the scope of their activities as defined in the Act on forests, forest protection and forestry,
- l) professional hunter within the scope of their activities as defined in the Act on the protection of game, game management and hunting,
- m) * fishery guard within the scope of their activities as defined in the Act on fish management and fish protection,
- n) person performing enforcement tasks at a business organization operating public transport vehicles in the course of this activity,
- o) person performing customer service tasks at the universal postal service provider in the course of this activity,
- p) * school guard within the scope of their activities as defined in the Act on the Police,
- q) * armed security guard in the performance of their service,
- r) * person performing monitoring of the proper operation of parking spaces and the proper use of parking spaces at an organization providing parking public services aimed at ensuring parking with road vehicles on national and local public roads, on private roads not closed to public traffic owned by the state or local government, and in squares, parks and other public spaces, pursuant to the Act on road traffic, in the course of this activity,
- s) * road inspector, and manager's inspector within the scope of their activities as defined in the Act on road traffic;
  - 13. foreign official person:
- a) * person performing legislative, judicial, administrative or law enforcement tasks in a foreign state, or person performing public authority tasks or providing service in a foreign state, including a person performing such tasks or providing such service in a public body or in a state or municipal enterprise,
- b) person providing service at an international organization established by an international treaty promulgated by law, whose activity forms part of the proper functioning of the organization,
- c) person elected to the assembly or body of an international organization established by an international treaty promulgated by law, including a Member of the European Parliament elected abroad,
- d) judge of an international court having jurisdiction over the territory of Hungary or its nationals, person providing service at the international court whose activity forms part of the proper functioning of the court;
  - 14. relative:
- a) lineal relative and their spouse or cohabitant,
- b) the adoptive parent and foster parent (including the cohabiting stepparent), the adopted and foster child (including the cohabiting stepchild),
- c) the sibling and the spouse or cohabitant of the sibling,
- d) the spouse, the cohabitant,
- e) the lineal relative and sibling of the spouse or cohabitant;
  - 15. information system: equipment ensuring the automatic processing, handling, storage, transmission of data, or the totality of such interconnected equipment;
  - 16. damage: unless otherwise provided by this Act, the decrease in value caused by the criminal offense to property;
  - 17. pecuniary disadvantage: unless otherwise provided by this Act, the damage caused to property and the foregone pecuniary advantage;
  - 18. * narcotic drug: substance listed in Schedule 1 and 2 of narcotic drugs in Annex 1 of BM Decree 78/2022 (XII. 28.), and in Schedule 1 and 2 of psychotropic substances in Annex 2;
  - 19. * cash-substitute payment instrument means the cash-substitute payment instrument as defined in the Act on credit institutions and the negotiable voucher, the treasury card, the traveller's cheque, the voucher issued by law for the purpose of settling the consideration for a limited range of goods or services that may be provided with or without tax charged to the payer, and the bill of exchange, provided that its design, encoding or the signature on it protects against copying, falsification or unauthorized use;
  - 20. electronic cash-substitute payment instrument means, in addition to the cash-substitute payment instrument as defined in the Act on credit institutions, the treasury card and the electronic voucher issued pursuant to the authorization of the Act on personal income tax, provided that these are used through an information system;
  - 21. public utility:
- a) public utility,
- b) public transport operation,
- c) electronic communications network,
- d) logistics, financial and IT centres and operations operated by the universal postal service provider to fulfil its tasks of public interest,
- e) plant producing military equipment, military technical equipment, plant producing energy or raw materials for industrial use;
  - 22. commission of the criminal offense in a large public forum shall also include commission of the criminal offense through publication in a press product, media service, by reproduction or by publication on an electronic communications network;
  - 23. weapon prohibited by international treaty:
- a) asphyxiating, poisonous and other similar gases, and bacteriological instruments referred to in the Protocol for the Prohibition of the Use in War of Asphyxiating, Poisonous or Other Gases, and of Bacteriological Methods of Warfare signed at Geneva on 17 June 1925, promulgated by Decree-Law No. 20 of 1955,
- b) bacteriological (biological) and toxin weapons as defined in Article 1 of the Convention on the Prohibition of the Development, Production and Stockpiling of Bacteriological (Biological) and Toxin Weapons and on their Destruction adopted at the XXVI session of the United Nations on 10 December 1971, promulgated by Decree-Law No. 11 of 1975,
- c) ca) weapon causing injury by non-detectable fragments as defined in Protocol I annexed to the Convention on Prohibitions or Restrictions on the Use of Certain Conventional Weapons Which May be Deemed to be Excessively Injurious or to Have Indiscriminate Effects signed at Geneva on 15 October 1980, promulgated by Decree-Law No. 2 of 1984, cb) mine, remotely delivered mine, anti-personnel mine, booby-trap and other device as defined in Article 2(1)-(5) of Amended Protocol II promulgated by Act CXXXIII of 1997,
  - cc) incendiary weapon as defined in Article 1(1) of Protocol III, cd) blinding laser weapon as defined in Article I of Protocol IV,
- d) chemical weapon or chemical riot control agent as defined in Article II(1) and (7) of the Convention on the Prohibition of the Development, Production, Stockpiling and Use of Chemical Weapons and on their Destruction signed at Paris on 13 January 1993, promulgated by Act CIV of 1997,
- e) anti-personnel mine as defined in Article 2(1) of the Convention on the Prohibition of the Use, Stockpiling, Production and Transfer of Anti-Personnel Mines and on their Destruction adopted at Oslo on 18 September 1997, promulgated by Act X of 1998,
- f) cluster munition as defined in Article 2(2) of the Convention on Cluster Munitions promulgated by Act XI of 2012, and explosive bomblet as defined in Article 2(13);
  - 24. * assembly: assembly falling within the scope of the Act on the right of assembly;

24a. * public event: an event, excluding assembly, that is open to everyone under equal conditions;

25. prostitution means engaging in sexual acts for the purpose of regular profit;

26. violent criminal offense against persons:

- a) genocide [Section 142(1)], crime against humanity [Section 143(1)], apartheid [Section 144(1)-(3)],
- b) violence against a war envoy (Section 148), violence against protected persons [Section 149(1)-(2) and (4)], other war crime (Section 158),
- c) homicide [Section 160(1)-(3) and (5)], homicide committed in a state of strong emotional disturbance (Section 161), bodily injury [Section 164(3)-(6) and (8)],
- d) * kidnapping [Section 190(1)-(4)], trafficking in human beings and forced labour [Section 192(1)-(6)], violation of personal liberty (Section 194), coercion (Section 195),
- e) * sexual coercion (Section 196), sexual violence [Section 197(1)-(4a)], domestic violence (Section 212/A),
- f) * violation of freedom of conscience and religion (Section 215), violence against a member of a community [Section 216(2)-(3)], violation of freedom of association and assembly [Section 217(1)],
- g) violent change of the constitutional order [Section 254(1)], insurrection [Section 256(1)-(2)],
- h) abuse in official proceedings [Section 301(1)-(2)], abuse in proceedings of a person performing a public duty [Section 302(1)-(2)], torture [Section 303(1)-(2)], unlawful detention (Section 304),
- i) violence against an official person [Section 310(1)-(3) and (5)], violence against a person performing a public duty (Section 311), violence against a supporter of an official person or person performing a public duty (Section 312), violence against an internationally protected person [Section 313(1)],
- j) act of terrorism [Section 314(1)-(2)], seizure of a vehicle [Section 320(1)-(2)],
- k) robbery [Section 365(1)-(4)], extortion (Section 367), taking the law into one's own hands [Section 368(1)-(2)],
- l) the qualified cases of mutiny [Section 442(2)-(6)] and violence against a superior or service officer [Section 445];
  - 27. sexual act: sexual intercourse and any seriously indecent act which is capable of, or is aimed at, arousing, maintaining or satisfying sexual desire;
  - 28. a person commits a criminal offense for profit if they seek regular gain through the commission of criminal offenses of the same or similar nature;
  - 29. * a person shall also be considered defenceless if, by reason of their situation or condition, they are temporarily or permanently incapable of offering resistance;
  - 30. protected cultural property shall also include cultural property declared protected;
  - 31. * a perpetrator of an intentional criminal offense is a recidivist if they were previously sentenced to executable imprisonment for an intentional criminal offense, and three years have not yet elapsed from the completion of the sentence or the termination of its enforceability until the commission of the new criminal offense;
- a) * special recidivist is a recidivist who commits a criminal offense of the same or similar nature on both occasions;
- b) multiple recidivist is a person who, prior to the commission of the intentional criminal offense, was sentenced as a recidivist to executable imprisonment, and three years have not yet elapsed from the completion of the last sentence or the termination of its enforceability until the commission of the new criminal offense punishable by imprisonment;
- c) * violent multiple recidivist is a multiple recidivist who commits a violent criminal offense against persons on all three occasions;
  - 32. * internationally protected person: a foreign official person who, under the international treaty applicable to them, enjoys immunity under international law;
  - 33. * new psychoactive substance: substance specified in Annex 3 of BM Decree 78/2022 (XII. 28.);
  - 34. * foreign protected cultural heritage element: pursuant to the Council of Europe Convention on Offences relating to Cultural Property, done at Nicosia on 19 May 2017, promulgated by Act CXXV of 2021,
- a) movable cultural heritage element classified, defined or designated in accordance with Article 2(2)(a), and
- b) immovable cultural heritage element defined or designated in accordance with Article 2(2)(b);
  - 35. * humanitarian operation: assistance activity, with the consent of the host country or the authorization of an international organization entitled thereto, carried out with the substantive participation of the Defence Forces, aimed at eliminating and reducing the consequences of a situation caused by armed conflict, natural or civilizational disaster that seriously threatens or damages human life, health, material values, basic supply, and the natural environment;
  - 36. * humanitarian activity conducted in a foreign theatre of operations: the participation of the Defence Forces in a territory endangered by armed clashes between opposing parties, for the purpose of ensuring the life, physical integrity, health and basic goods necessary for subsistence of the civilian population;
  - 37. * prohibited foreign support: support from abroad whose acceptance or use is prohibited by the Act on the operation and management of political parties or the Act on electoral procedure;
  - 38. * responsible person: the responsible person as defined in the Act on civil society organizations.
    - (2) Where this Act refers to a cohabitant, it shall also be understood to include a registered cohabitant.
    - (3) * Where this Act refers to a member of the Defence Forces, theatre of operations, allied armed forces or peacekeeping, the terms as defined in the Act on national defence and the Hungarian Defence Forces shall be understood.
    - (3a) * Where this Act refers to rank, in the case of a member of the Defence Forces, the rank held shall be understood, and in the case of professional staff members of the police, the Guard of the National Assembly, the penitentiary organization, the professional disaster management body and the civilian national security services, the rank determined on the basis of the classification of the service position held and the pay grade shall be understood.
    - (4) Where this Act refers to strictly confidential, confidential, restricted or limited distribution classified data, the national or foreign classified data as defined in the Act on the protection of classified data shall be understood.
    - (5) * For the purposes of this Act
- a) third-country national shall be understood as defined in the Act on the general rules concerning the entry and residence of third-country nationals,
- b) permit issued for the purpose of employment: ba) National Card, bb) Corporate Card issued pursuant to Section 48(2)(b) or (c) of Act XC of 2023 on the general rules concerning the entry and residence of third-country nationals, bc) residence permit for seasonal employment, bd) residence permit for employment purposes issued for the purpose of realizing an investment, be) residence permit for employment purposes, bf) guest worker residence permit, bg) Hungarian Card, bh) EU Blue Card, bi) residence permit issued for the purpose of intra-corporate transfer (including the long-term mobility permit), bj) residence permit for research purposes (including the researcher long-term residence permit, and the researcher short-term mobility certificate), bk) residence permit for study purposes (including the student mobility residence permit, and the student mobility certificate), bl) residence permit for the purpose of ensuring family unity issued in a combined application procedure, bm) residence permit issued for humanitarian purposes, bn) residence permit for posting purposes.
  - (6) For the purposes of this Act, the value, damage and pecuniary disadvantage
- a) between fifty thousand and one forint and five hundred thousand forints is minor,
- b) between five hundred thousand and one forint and five million forints is greater,
- c) between five million and one forint and fifty million forints is significant,
- d) between fifty million and one forint and five hundred million forints is particularly large,
- e) above five hundred million forints is particularly significant.

## 460. §

(1) For the purposes of Chapter XIV, a protected person is one who does not take a direct part in hostilities, in particular

a) a member of the armed forces who has laid down their weapons and surrendered, withdrawing themselves from hostilities;

b) a person who has clearly become incapable of fighting as a result of illness, injury, capture or other reason, or has clearly withdrawn themselves from hostilities;

c) personnel of a humanitarian aid or peacekeeping mission operating under the Charter of the United Nations, if they are entitled to the protection afforded to civilian persons or civilian objects by the international law of armed conflict;d) a civilian, insofar as and for as long as they do not take a direct part in hostilities.

(2) For the purposes of Chapter XXVI

a) a disciplinary matter shall be considered a procedure for which the law establishes the conduct constituting a disciplinary offense, the detailed rules of the procedure and the disciplinary penalties that may be imposed;

b) a civil matter shall also include a case pending before an arbitral tribunal.

## 461. §

(1) For the purposes of §§ 176–180, a drug is a small quantity if

a) its pure active substance content given in base form is aa) in the case of LSD, 0.001 grams, ab) in the case of psilocybin, 0.1 grams, ac) in the case of psilocin, 0.2 grams, ad) * in the case of amphetamine, methamphetamine, MDPV and alpha-PVP, 0.5 grams, ae) in the case of dihydrocodeine, 0.8 grams, af) in the case of heroin, 0.6 grams, ag) in the case of morphine, 0.9 grams, ah) in the case of ketamine, codeine, MDA, MDMA, N-ethyl-MDA (MDE), MBDB, 1-PEA, N-methyl-1-PEA, mCPP, methadone, 4-fluoroamphetamine and pethidine, 1 gram, ai) in the case of mephedrone, methylone and 4-MEC, 1.5 grams, aj) in the case of cocaine, 2 grams, ak) in the case of BZP, 3 grams, al) * in the case of pentedrone, 0.4 grams,

b) in the case of GHB, its pure active substance content given in acid form is 7.5 grams,

c) in the case of tetrahydrocannabinol (THC), the pure and acid form THC content present together (total THC) is 6 grams,

d) * its pure active substance content da) in the case of GBL, is 6.2 grams, db) in the case of AB–CHMINACA, MDMB–CHMICA, AM–2201, AB–PINACA, AB–FUBINACA, and ADB–FUBINACA, does not exceed 0.05 grams.

(2) For the purposes of §§ 176–180, a drug is a small quantity if, in the case of cannabis plant, the number of plant individuals is at most five.

(3) For the purposes of §§ 176–180, the drug according to paragraphs (1)–(2)

a) is a significant quantity if it exceeds twenty times the upper limit of the small quantity determined for the given drug,

b) is a particularly significant quantity if it exceeds two hundred times the upper limit of the small quantity determined for the given drug.

(4) For the purposes of §§ 176–180, in the case of drugs not listed in paragraphs (1)–(2), the drug

a) is a small quantity if its pure active substance content does not exceed seven times the average effective dose for an unhabituated consumer,

b) is a significant quantity if its pure active substance content exceeds one hundred and forty times the average effective dose for an unhabituated consumer,

c) is a particularly significant quantity if its pure active substance content exceeds one thousand four hundred times the average effective dose for an unhabituated consumer.

(5) * For the purposes of §§ 184–184/C, a new psychoactive substance is a small quantity if its pure active substance content in the list contained in Annex 3 of BM Decree 78/2022. (XII. 28.) *

a) in the case of the group of compounds according to point 1, does not exceed 0.5 grams,

b) in the case of the group of compounds according to point 2, does not exceed 1.5 grams,

c) in the case of the group of compounds according to point 3, does not exceed 2 grams,

d) in the case of the group of compounds according to point 4, does not exceed 1 gram,

e) in the case of the group of compounds according to point 5, does not exceed 0.1 grams,

f) in the case of substances according to point 6, does not exceed 2 grams.

(6) * For the purposes of §§ 184–184/C, a new psychoactive substance is a significant quantity if its pure active substance content exceeds twenty times the upper limit of the small quantity.

(7) * For the purposes of §§ 184/B and 184/C, a new psychoactive substance is a particularly significant quantity if its pure active substance content exceeds two hundred times the upper limit of the small quantity.

(8) * In the case of compounds occurring in salt form, pure active substance content shall be understood as the active substance content given in base form.

## 462. § (1) No criminal offense is committed if

a) negligent handling causes pecuniary damage not exceeding one hundred thousand forints,

b) abuse of social security, social or other welfare benefits is committed causing damage not exceeding fifty thousand forints.

(2) Not a criminal offense, but a petty offense is committed if

a) damage and fraud are committed causing damage not exceeding fifty thousand forints,

b) * theft, embezzlement and misappropriation are committed for a value not exceeding fifty thousand forints,

c) breach of trust is committed causing pecuniary damage not exceeding fifty thousand forints,

d) infringement of copyright or rights related to copyright is committed with respect to da) blank media fees and reprographic fees not exceeding one hundred thousand forints, db) causing pecuniary damage not exceeding one hundred thousand forints,

e) infringement of industrial property rights is committed causing pecuniary damage not exceeding one hundred thousand forints,

f) * placing a product of poor quality on the market or imitation of a competitor is committed for a value not exceeding one hundred thousand forints,

g) * money laundering is committed for a value not exceeding fifty thousand forints, according to the provisions of § 177 (1a) of Act II of 2012 on Petty Offenses, Petty Offense Proceedings and the Petty Offense Registration System.

(3) * No criminal offense is committed, or a customs petty offense is committed, if the pecuniary damage caused by budget fraud does not exceed five hundred thousand forints.

(4) * A criminal offense is committed if, in the case of acts committed by the same perpetrator within one year and adjudicated together

a) * in the case of the same petty offense against property, the value of the thing, the damage caused, or the pecuniary damage caused exceeds the amount specified in paragraph (2) points a)–c) or g),

b) in the case of infringement of copyright or rights related to copyright, the blank media fee or reprographic fee exceeds the amount specified in paragraph (2) point d),

c) in the case of infringement of industrial property rights, the pecuniary damage exceeds the amount specified in paragraph (2) point e),

d) in the case of placing a product of poor quality on the market or imitation of a competitor, the value of the product or goods exceeds the amount specified in paragraph (2) point f),

e) in the case of a customs petty offense, the pecuniary damage exceeds the amount specified in paragraph (3) as a result of cumulation of values.

(5) * A criminal offense is committed in the case of a petty offense of infringement of copyright or rights related to copyright committed by the same perpetrator and adjudicated together, if the individual acts infringe the copyright or related rights of another or others existing under the law on copyright, causing pecuniary damage exceeding the amount specified in paragraph (2) point d).

463. § This Act enters into force on 1 July 2013.
